We begin by stating our own variant of the now-ubiquitous performance difference lemma for LQR, which we use to bound the suboptimality of a controller
K
in terms of its Hilbert-Schmidt difference from
K_{\star}
, weighted by the initial exploration covariance
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}
. This result follows by combining the standard LQR performance difference lemma with our change of measure result,
Lemma

2.1
.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
K
be a stabilizing controller for
A_{\star},B_{\star}
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(K)-\mathcal{J}(K_{\star})~\leq~\mathcal{C}_{K,\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}\left\|R+B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert(K-K_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

for
\mathcal{C}_{K,\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}=\max\left\{2,\frac{128}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|K-K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|P_{K}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\log\left(3\left\|P_{K}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)^{2}\right\}
for
P_{K}=P_{\infty}(K;A_{\star},B_{\star})
defined as in
Lemma

2.1
.

Proof.

The proof follows by applying
Lemma

2.1
on the standard performance difference lemma for LQR. By Lemma 12 in
Fazel et al. 2018
(as presented in Lemma 4 from
Mania et al. 2019
),

\displaystyle J_{\star}(K)-J_{\star}(K_{\star})=\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\star}(K)(K-K_{\star})^{\mathsf{H}}(R+B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star})(K-K_{\star})\right].

Now, by
Lemma

2.1
,
\Sigma_{\star}(K)\preceq\mathcal{C}_{K,\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}\cdot\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}.
, and therefore

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\star}(K)(K-K_{\star})^{\mathsf{H}}(R+B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star})(K-K_{\star})\right]

\displaystyle\quad~\leq~\mathcal{C}_{K,\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}(K-K_{\star})^{\mathsf{H}}(R+B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star})(K-K_{\star})\right]

\displaystyle\quad~\leq~\mathcal{C}_{K,\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}\left\|R+B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert(K-K_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}.

∎

C.1.2
Intermediate
K
Perturbation

Next, we give a bound controlling the error
\|(K_{\star}-\widehat{K})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}
in terms of the maximum of the errors,

\displaystyle\max\left\{\|\widehat{B}-B_{\star}\|_{\mathsf{HS}},\|(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}},\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}\right\}.

The following proposition is proven in
Section

C.3
.

Proposition C.2
.

Recall our earlier definitions,
\widehat{P}=P_{\infty}(\widehat{A},\widehat{B}),\widehat{K}=K_{\infty}(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})
. Assume that the following inequality holds for some
\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}~\geq~1
, and
\varepsilon~\leq~1
,

\displaystyle\max\left\{\|\widehat{B}-B_{\star}\|_{\mathsf{HS}},\|(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}},\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}\right\}~\leq~\varepsilon.

Furthermore, suppose
(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})
is also stabilizable, let
P_{0}=P_{\infty}(K_{0};A_{\star},B_{\star})
, and set

\displaystyle M_{K}=\max\left\{\left\|A_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2},\left\|B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2},\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\widehat{P}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|P_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathrm{op}},1\right\},

be a uniform bound on the operator norm on relevant operators. Then,

\displaystyle\left\lVert(K_{\star}-\widehat{K})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}~\leq~9\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}\varepsilon M_{K}^{4}.

The two challenges in applying
Proposition

C.2
are (a) verifying that the nominal system
(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})
is stabilizable, so that the uniform bound
M
is finite, and (b) bounding the weighted error
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}
. We address these two parts of the argument in what follows.

C.1.3
Operator-Norm
P
-Perturbation

Proposition C.3
.

Fix two instances
(A_{1},B_{1})
,
(A_{2},B_{2})
, and define
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}=\max\{\|B_{1}-B_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|A_{1}-A_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}
. Suppose
P_{1}=P_{\infty}(A_{1},B_{1})
,
K_{1}:=K_{\infty}(A_{1},B_{1})
, and fix a tolerance parameter
\eta\in(0,1]
. Then,

•

If
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta/(16\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3})
, then
P_{2}=P_{\infty}(A_{2},B_{2})
and
P_{\infty}(K_{1};A_{2},B_{2})
are bounded operators, and

\displaystyle P_{2}\preceq P_{\infty}(K_{1};A_{2},B_{2})\preceq P_{1}+\eta\|P_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}I\preceq(1+\|P_{1}\|)\eta P_{1}

(C.1)

•

If the stronger condition
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta/(16(1+\eta)^{4}\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3})
holds, then,
\|P_{2}-P_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta\|P_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
.

The proof is deferred to
Section

C.4
. One important consequence of the above bound is that, by setting
\eta=1/11
,
Proposition

C.3
shows that the closeness condition,
2.1
, implies that
\|\widehat{P}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1.2\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
(see
Lemma

C.8
). This is an essential ingredient in the subsequent covariance-weighted bound.

C.1.4
Covariance-Weighted Perturbation of
P_{\star}

To state the covariance-weighted perturbation of
P_{\star}
, we recall the uniform closeness condition:
2.1
.

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathsf{op}}:=\max\left\{\left\|\widehat{A}-A_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}},\left\|\widehat{B}-B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\}~\leq~\frac{1}{229\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}}

(C.2)

For the following proposition, we assume access to a stabilizing controller
K_{0}
, and define
P_{0}:=\left\|P_{\infty}(K_{0};A_{\star},B_{\star})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. We set
M_{P_{\star}}:=\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
and
M_{P_{0}}=\|P_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

Proposition C.4
.

Suppose
\widehat{A},\widehat{B}
satisfy
2.1
and recall our earlier definition
\widehat{P}=P_{\infty}(\widehat{A},\widehat{B}).
Then,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{P}\cdot\varepsilon_{P}\cdot\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(\kappa_{P}\right)}\cdot\phi(\kappa_{P})^{\alpha_{\mathrm{op}}},

where
\phi(u):=e^{\sqrt{\log(u)}}
and
\varepsilon_{P},\mathcal{C}_{P},\kappa_{P},\alpha_{\mathrm{op}}
are defined as:

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{P}^{2}

\displaystyle:=2\|(A_{\star}-\widehat{A})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+2.4M_{P_{\star}}\|B_{\star}-\widehat{B}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{P}

\displaystyle:=M_{P_{0}}^{4}M_{P_{\star}}^{3/2}\sqrt{\left(1+\smash{\tfrac{\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}}\right)\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}

\displaystyle\kappa_{P}

\displaystyle:=1+2\smash{\tfrac{\|\widehat{A}-A_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]}{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle:=2M_{P_{\star}}^{5/2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1/100.

Furthermore, the above bound holds for any
\varepsilon_{P}^{\prime}~\geq~\varepsilon_{P}
. For the case of finite-dimensional systems where
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\succ 0
,
\kappa_{P}
can be replaced by
1+\mathrm{cond}(\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0})
, where
\mathrm{cond}(\cdot)
denotes the condition number.

Proposition

C.4
is the most involved and technically innovative in our analysis. Its proof is presented in
Section

C.5
.

C.2
Proof of the End-to-End Perturbation Bound:
Theorem

2.1

We recall:

\displaystyle M_{\star}:=\max\{\left\|A_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2},\left\|B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2},\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}},\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}},1\}.

(C.3)

Proof.

The proof follows by combining the performance difference lemma (
Lemma

C.1
), our controller perturbation bound (
Proposition

C.2
), and the riccati perturbation bound (
Proposition

C.4
).

Applying performance difference

Starting from the performance difference lemma, we have that

\displaystyle J(\widehat{K})-J(K_{\star})~\leq~\mathcal{C}_{\widehat{K},\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}\left\|R+B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert(\widehat{K}-K_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

for
\mathcal{C}_{\widehat{K},\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}=\max\left\{2,\frac{128}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\widehat{K}-K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|P_{\widehat{K}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\log\left(2\left\|P_{\widehat{K}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)^{2}\right\}
where
P_{\widehat{K}}=P_{\infty}(\widehat{K};A_{\star},B_{\star})
.

We now simplify these terms. Since
K_{0}=K_{\infty}(A_{0},B_{0})
and
\widehat{K}=K_{\infty}(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})
where both pairs of systems
(A_{0},B_{0})
,
(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})
satisfy the uniform closeness conditions, we can apply
Lemma

C.8
to conclude that

\displaystyle P_{\infty}(\widehat{K};A_{\star},B_{\star})\preceq 1.2P_{\star},\quad\left\|P_{\infty}(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}~\leq~1.1\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}},\quad\text{ and }\left\|P_{\infty}(A_{0},B_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}~\leq~1.1\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

Furthermore, by
Lemma

D.7
,
\|\widehat{K}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}~\leq~\|P_{\infty}(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})\|_{\mathrm{op}}
and similarly,
\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}~\leq~\left\|P_{\infty}(A_{0},B_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{K}-K_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}~\leq~2(\|\widehat{K}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}+\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2})~\leq~4.4\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

Recalling that
M_{\star}=\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}~\geq~1
and
\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}~\geq~1
,

\frac{128}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\widehat{K}-K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|P_{\widehat{K}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\log\left(2\left\|P_{\widehat{K}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)^{2}\lesssim M_{\star}^{5}\log(M_{\star})^{2}.

Therefore, under the uniform closeness assumptions, we have that,

\displaystyle J(\widehat{K})-J(K_{\star})\lesssim M_{\star}^{7}\log(M_{\star})^{2}\left\lVert(\widehat{K}-K_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2},

(C.4)

where we have used the calculation
\left\|R+B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}~\leq~\left\|R\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\|B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim M_{\star}^{2}
.

Controller perturbation

Now, we include our controller perturbation bound (
Proposition

C.2
) which states that for
M_{K}
defined as,

\displaystyle M_{K}=\max\left\{\left\|A_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2},\left\|B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2},\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\widehat{P}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|P_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathrm{op}},1\right\},

we have that

\displaystyle\left\lVert(K_{\star}-\widehat{K})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}~\leq~9\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}\varepsilon M_{K}^{4}.

for

\varepsilon=\max\left\{\left\lVert\widehat{B}-B_{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2},\left\lVert(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2},\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\right\}.

Under the uniform operator norm closeness, we again have
\|\widehat{P}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim M_{\star}
, and similarly for
P_{0}
.
Therefore,
M_{K}
as defined in
Proposition

C.2
satisfies
M_{K}\lesssim M_{\star}
. Using the controller perturbation, we then conclude that,

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\widehat{K}-K_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\lesssim\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}M_{\star}^{9}\log(M_{\star})^{2}\varepsilon.

(C.5)

Riccati perturbation

The last step in the proof is to apply our Riccati perturbation bound from
Proposition

C.4
. The bound states that,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle~\leq~\mathcal{C}_{P}^{2}\cdot\varepsilon_{P}^{2}\cdot\log_{+}\left(\kappa_{P}\right)\cdot\phi(\kappa_{P})^{1/50}

where
\phi(u):=e^{\sqrt{\log(u)}}
, and the remaining quantities are defined as,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{P}^{2}

\displaystyle:=2\|(A_{\star}-\widehat{A})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+2.4\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert B_{\star}-\widehat{B}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{P}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|P_{\infty}(A_{0},B_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{4}\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2}\sqrt{\left(1+\smash{\tfrac{\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}}\right)\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}

\displaystyle\kappa_{P}

\displaystyle:=1+2\smash{\tfrac{\|\widehat{A}-A_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]}{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}}

We note that by
2.1
,
\left\|P_{\infty}(A_{0},B_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. Furthermore, by
Lemma

A.1
and the fact
\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;I\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0},\;I\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle~\leq~M_{\star}(\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}M_{\star}),

where we have used the fact that
I\preceq Q
and hence

\left\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0},\;I\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}~\leq~\left\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0},\;Q+K_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}RK_{\star}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

Using this calculation, we can then bound the relevant quantities as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{P}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim M_{\star}^{11}(1+\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}/\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}})\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}M_{\star}^{15}

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{P}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim M_{\star}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\log_{+}(\kappa_{P})

\displaystyle~\leq~\log\left(e+\frac{2e\|\widehat{A}-A_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right):=\mathcal{L},

where we also note that
Proposition

C.4
allows us to replace
\mathcal{L}
by
\log(1+\mathrm{cond}(\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}))
in finite dimension with
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\succ 0
. Using these simplifications, we get that,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}M_{\star}^{18}\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}\exp(\frac{1}{50}\sqrt{\mathcal{L}})\varepsilon^{2}.

(C.6)

Wrapping up

Combining this last equation with the bounds from
Equation

C.4
and
Equation

C.5
, the total power of
M_{\star}
is
M_{\star}^{(7+9+18)}\log(M_{\star})^{2}~\leq~M_{\star}^{36}
, yielding:

\displaystyle J(\widehat{K})-J(K_{\star})\lesssim\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}^{4}M_{\star}^{36}\mathcal{L}^{1/2}\exp(\frac{1}{50}\sqrt{\mathcal{L}})\varepsilon^{2}.

∎

C.3
Proof of Intermediate
K
Perturbation:
Proposition

C.2

Recall the definition

\displaystyle M_{K}=\max\left\{\left\|A_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2},\left\|B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2},\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\widehat{P}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|P_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathrm{op}},1,\right\},

where
P_{0}=P_{\infty}(K_{0};A_{\star},B_{\star})
. To simplify notation, we use
M=M_{K}
. Furthermore, we assume
\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}~\geq~1
, as is chosen in our algorithm later on. Our proof appeals to the following lemma from
Mania et al. 2019
:

Lemma C.5

(
Mania et al. 2019
)
.

Let
f_{1},f_{2}
be
\gamma
-strongly convex functions. Let
\mathbf{x}_{i}=\argmin f_{i}(\mathbf{x})
for
i=1,2
. If
\|\nabla\mkern-2.5muf_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{2})\|~\leq~\varepsilon
then,
\|\mathbf{x}_{1}-\mathbf{x}_{2}\|~\leq~\varepsilon/\gamma

Proof of
Proposition

C.2
.

The proof is inspired by that of Lemma 2 in
Mania et al. 2019
. Consider the functions
f^{\star}
and
\widehat{f}
defined as,

\displaystyle f^{\star}(X)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert R^{1/2}X\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert P_{\star}^{1/2}B_{\star}X\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+\left\langle B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}A_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2},\;X\right\rangle_{\mathsf{HS}}

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(X)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert R^{1/2}X\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert\widehat{P}^{1/2}\widehat{B}X\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+\left\langle\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{A}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2},\;X\right\rangle_{\mathsf{HS}},

where
\left\langle A,\;B\right\rangle_{\mathsf{HS}}=\mathrm{tr}\left[A^{\mathsf{H}}B\right]
denotes the Hilbert-Schmidt inner product.
Both functions are strongly convex with strong convexity parameter lower bounded by
\sigma_{\min}(R)~\geq~1
. We observe that,

\displaystyle\nabla\mkern-2.5muf^{\star}(X)

\displaystyle=(B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star}+R)X+B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}A_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}.

\displaystyle\nabla\mkern-2.5mu\widehat{f}(X)

\displaystyle=(\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{B}+R)X+\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{A}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}.

and hence,

\displaystyle X_{\star}

\displaystyle=\argmin_{X}f^{\star}(X)=-(B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star}+R)^{-1}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}A_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}=K_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\widehat{X}

\displaystyle=\argmin_{X}\widehat{f}(X)=-(\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{B}+R)^{-1}\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{A}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}=\widehat{K}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}.

Next, we show that the norm of the difference between both gradients is small. We will repeatedly use the fact that
\left\lVert AB\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}~\leq~\left\|A\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert B\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}
(and similarly
\left\lVert AB\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}~\leq~\left\|B\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}
) throughout the remainder of the proof.

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla\mkern-2.5muf^{\star}(X)-\nabla\mkern-2.5mu\widehat{f}(X)\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}~\leq~\left\lVert B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star}-\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{B}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\left\|X\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert(B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}A_{\star}-\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{A})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}.

(C.7)

Bounding the first term in the above decomposition,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{B}-B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}B_{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\left\lVert\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{B}-B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}B_{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}+\left\lVert B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})B_{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\lVert\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{B}\pm\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}B_{\star}-B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}B_{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}+\varepsilon

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\lVert\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}+\left\lVert(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})\widehat{P}B_{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}+\varepsilon

\displaystyle~\leq~4M^{3/2}\varepsilon,

where in the last line, we used
\|\widehat{B}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\varepsilon+\|B_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\sqrt{M}
, since
\varepsilon\leq 1
and
M\geq\max\{1,\|B_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\}
. In inequality
(i)
, we used the following calculation:

\displaystyle\left\lVert B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})B_{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\left[B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})B_{\star}\right]

\displaystyle~\leq~\mathrm{tr}\left[B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})B_{\star}\right]

(
B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\preceq\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}
)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle~\leq~\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right].

The last line is equal to
\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}
which is less than
\varepsilon^{2}
by assumption. Next, we bound the second term in
Equation

C.7
,

\left\lVert(\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{A}-B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{\star}A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}~\leq~\underbrace{\left\lVert(\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{A}-B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}}_{T_{1}}+\underbrace{\left\lVert B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})A_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}}_{T_{2}}.

We bound
T_{1}
as follows:

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}\widehat{A}\pm\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}A_{\star}-B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\lVert\widehat{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}+\left\lVert(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})^{\mathsf{H}}\widehat{P}A_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\varepsilon\left\|\widehat{B}\widehat{P}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\varepsilon\left\|\widehat{P}A_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left(2M^{3/2}+M^{3/2}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)\varepsilon,

where again we used
\|\widehat{B}\|\leq\sqrt{2M}
. Before bounding
T_{2}
, we observe that:

\displaystyle A_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}A_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}

\displaystyle\preceq 2(A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}(A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}}+2(B_{\star}K_{0})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}(B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}}

\displaystyle\preceq 2\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}+2\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}

\displaystyle\preceq 2(1+\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}.

(C.8)

To go from the first to the second line, we have used the fact that since

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}=\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right),

by definition of
\mathsf{dlyap}
,

\displaystyle(A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}(A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}}=\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}-B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}-\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\preceq\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}.

Applying (
C.8
), we can now bound
T_{2}
using a similar calculation as before,

\displaystyle\left\lVert B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})A_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\left[B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})A_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}A_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})B_{\star}\right]

\displaystyle~\leq~2(1+\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}})\mathrm{tr}\left[B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})B_{\star}\right]

\displaystyle=2(1+\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}})\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(\widehat{P}-P_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle~\leq~2(1+\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}})\varepsilon^{2}.

Returning to
Equation

C.7
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\mkern-2.5muf^{\star}(X)-\nabla\mkern-2.5mu_{\mathbf{u}}\widehat{f}(X)\|

\displaystyle~\leq~5M^{3/2}\varepsilon\left\|X\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(2M^{3/2}+M^{3/2}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\sqrt{2(1+\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}})}\right)\varepsilon,

and for
X=X_{\star}=K_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}
, we have that,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\mkern-2.5mu\widehat{f}(X_{\star})\|

\displaystyle~\leq~4M^{3/2}\varepsilon\left\|K_{\star}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle+\left(2M^{3/2}+M^{3/2}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\sqrt{2(1+\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}})}\right)\varepsilon.

We now simplify the above. By
Lemma

D.7
, we can take
\left\|K_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\leq M^{1/2}
, and
\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\left\|P_{K_{0}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\leq M^{1/2}
, where
P_{K_{0}}=P_{\infty}(K_{0};A_{\star},B_{\star})
. Moreover,
Lemma

D.9
yields
\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\left\|P_{K_{0}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}(\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}^{2}\left\|B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2})\leq\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}M^{3}
(since
\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}\geq 1
). Hence,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\mkern-2.5mu\widehat{f}(X_{\star})\|\leq 4M^{7/2}\varepsilon+(2M^{3/2}+\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}M^{5/2}+\sqrt{2(1+\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}M^{4})})\varepsilon\leq 9\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}M^{4}\varepsilon.

Lastly, by
Lemma

C.5
,

\left\lVert X_{\star}-\widehat{X}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}=\left\lVert(K_{\star}-\widehat{K})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}~\leq~9\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}M^{4}\varepsilon\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(R)}.

The precise statement follows by applying our assumption that
\sigma_{\min}(R)>1
.
∎

C.4
Proof of Operator Norm
P
-Perturbation:
Proposition

C.3

We begin the proof with the following lemma, which ensures that the Lyapunov function of a stable matrix
A_{1}
is also a Lyapunov function for a sufficiently nearby matrix
A_{2}
:

Lemma C.6
.

Let
A_{1},A_{2}
be two matrices with
A_{1}
stable. Set
P_{1}=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{1},\;\Sigma\right)
, where
\Sigma\succeq I
. Fix an
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and suppose that
\|A_{1}-A_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}}
. Then,
A_{2}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{1}A_{2}\preceq P_{1}(1-\frac{1-\alpha}{\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}})
, and iterating,

\displaystyle(A_{2}^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}P_{1}A_{2}^{j}\preceq P_{1}(1-\frac{1-\alpha}{\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}})^{j},\quad\forall j\geq 0.

Proof of
Lemma

C.6
.

From
Lemma

D.9
,
A_{1}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{1}A_{1}\preceq P_{1}(1-\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})
. Set
\Delta=A_{2}-A_{1}
. For any
\tau>0
, invoking
Lemma

D.3
,

\displaystyle A_{2}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{1}A_{2}

\displaystyle\preceq(A_{1}+\Delta)^{\mathsf{H}}P_{1}(A_{1}+\Delta)

\displaystyle=A_{1}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{1}A_{1}+\Delta^{\mathsf{H}}P_{1}\Delta+\Delta^{\mathsf{H}}P_{1}A_{1}+A_{1}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{1}\Delta

\displaystyle\preceq(1+\tau)A_{1}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{1}A_{1}+\left(1+\frac{1}{\tau}\right)\Delta^{\mathsf{H}}P_{1}\Delta

\displaystyle\preceq(1+\tau)P_{1}(1-\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})+\left(1+\frac{1}{\tau}\right)\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\preceq P_{1}\left\{(1-\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})(1+\tau)+\left(1+\frac{1}{\tau}\right)\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\},

where the last line follows from the fact that
P_{1}\succeq I
since
Q\succeq I
. Using our assumption that
\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}}
and optimizing over
\tau
, a short calculation shows that the expression between brackets above is bounded by
(1-(1-\alpha)/\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}})
. ∎

The next proposition provides a perturbation bound for the
\mathsf{dlyap}
operator:

Proposition C.7
.

Let
A_{1}
be a linear operator,
\Sigma\succeq I
, and
P_{1}=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{1},\;\Sigma\right)
. Define
\|\cdot\|_{\circ}
to be any spectral norm
(i.e.
\circ\in\{\mathrm{op},\mathsf{HS},\mathsf{tr}\}
)
. Then, for any
\alpha\in[0,1)
, linear operator
A_{2}
with
\|A_{1}-A_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}}
, and any symmetric
\Sigma_{0}
,
\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{2},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)
is a bounded operator, and

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{2},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)-\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{1},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)\|_{\circ}\leq 2\mathcal{C}_{\circ}\left\|A_{1}-A_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{7/2}(1-\alpha)^{-2},

where
\mathcal{C}_{\circ}=\|P_{1}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{0}P_{1}^{-1/2}\|_{\circ}
.

Proof of
Proposition

C.7
.

Set
\Delta=A_{1}-A_{2}
, and define the terms
E_{n}:=P_{1}^{1/2}(A_{1}^{n}-A_{2}^{n})
,
\gamma:=1-\frac{1-\alpha}{\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}
.
Since
A_{1},A_{2}
satisfy the necessary closeness conditions, by
Lemmas

D.9
and
C.6
, we have that,

\displaystyle\max\{\|P_{1}^{1/2}A_{1}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|P_{2}^{1/2}A_{1}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}\leq\sqrt{\|P_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\gamma^{n}}.

(C.9)

Next, by closed form expression for
\mathsf{dlyap}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{2},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)-\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{1},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geq 0}(A_{2}^{n})^{\mathsf{H}}\Sigma_{0}A_{2}^{n}-(A_{1}^{n})^{\mathsf{H}}\Sigma_{0}A_{1}^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geq 1}(A_{2}^{n}-A_{1}^{n})^{\mathsf{H}}\Sigma_{0}A_{2}^{n}+A_{1}^{n}\Sigma_{0}(A_{2}^{n}-A_{1}^{n})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{n\geq 1}E_{n}^{\mathsf{H}}P_{1}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{0}A_{2}^{n}+(A_{1}^{n})^{\mathsf{H}}\Sigma_{0}P_{1}^{-1/2}E_{n},

and thus,

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{2},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)-\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{1},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)\|_{\circ}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n\geq 1}\|E_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left(\|P_{1}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{0}A_{2}^{n}\|_{\circ}+\|(A_{1}^{n})^{\mathsf{H}}\Sigma_{0}P_{1}^{-1/2}\|_{\circ}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n\geq 1}\|E_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|P_{1}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{0}P_{1}^{-1/2}\|_{\circ}\left(\|P_{1}^{1/2}A_{2}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|P_{1}^{1/2}A_{1}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\|P_{1}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{0}P_{1}^{-1/2}\|_{\circ}}_{:=\mathcal{C}_{\circ}}\sum_{n\geq 1}\|E_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left(\|P_{1}^{1/2}A_{2}^{n}\|+\|P_{1}^{1/2}A_{1}^{n}\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{C}_{\circ}\sum_{n\geq 1}\|E_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\gamma^{n}}.

(
Equation

C.9
)

Next, we bound
\|E_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. Using the identity,
A_{1}^{n}-A_{2}^{n}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}A_{1}^{i}\Delta A_{2}^{n-i-1}
,

\displaystyle\|E_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n}\|P_{1}^{1/2}A_{1}^{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|A_{2}^{n-i-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n}\|P_{1}^{1/2}A_{1}^{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|P_{1}^{1/2}A_{2}^{n-i-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}},

(
P_{1}\succeq I
)

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|P_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{i=0}^{n}\sqrt{\gamma^{n-1}}.

(
Equation

C.9
)

Hence, combining the above,

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{2},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)-\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{1},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)\|_{\circ}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{C}_{\circ}\left\|\Delta\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2}\sum_{n\geq 1}n\gamma^{n-1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{C}_{\circ}\left\|\Delta\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2}\sum_{n\geq 1}n\gamma^{n-1}

(
\gamma\leq 1
)

\displaystyle=2\mathcal{C}_{\circ}\left\|\Delta\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2}(1-\gamma)^{-2}.

Substituting in
\gamma=(1-\frac{1-\alpha}{\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}})
, the above becomes
2\mathcal{C}_{\circ}\left\|\Delta\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{7/2}(1-\alpha)^{-2}
, concluding the proof.
∎

We may now conclude the proof of
Proposition

C.3

Proof of
Proposition

C.3
.

We prove the each part of the proposition individually.

Part 1

Define
\Sigma_{1}:=K_{1}^{\top}RK_{1}+Q
. Then,

\displaystyle P_{2}

\displaystyle\preceq P_{\infty}(K_{1};A_{2},B_{2})=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{2}+B_{2}K_{1},\;\Sigma_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\preceq\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{1}+B_{1}K_{1},\;\Sigma_{1}\right)+I\cdot\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{1}+B_{1}K_{1},\;\Sigma_{1}\right)-\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{2}+B_{2}K_{1},\;\Sigma_{1}\right)\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=P_{1}+I\cdot\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{1}+B_{1}K_{1},\;\Sigma_{1}\right)-\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{2}+B_{2}K_{1},\;\Sigma_{1}\right)\|_{\mathrm{op}}

(C.10)

If
\varepsilon_{0}^{2}:=\|A_{1}+B_{1}K_{1}-(A_{2}+B_{2}K_{1})\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{16\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}}
, then, invoking
Proposition

C.7
with
\Sigma_{0}\leftarrow\Sigma_{1}
,
\|\cdot\|_{\circ}\leftarrow\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
,
\alpha\leftarrow 1/2
, and noting how
\mathcal{C}_{\circ}\leq 1
, we can conclude that,

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{1}+B_{1}K_{1},\;\Sigma_{1}\right)-\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{2}+B_{2}K_{1},\;\Sigma_{1}\right)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 8\varepsilon_{0}\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{7/2}.

Observe that if
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\eta/(8\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{5/2})
for some
\eta\in(0,1]
, then (since
\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq 1
by
Lemma

D.7
) it holds that
\varepsilon_{0}^{2}~\leq~\frac{1}{64\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}}
. Plugging into the inequality above, we get our desired result,

P_{2}\preceq P_{1}+\eta\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot I.

Therefore, to finish the proof of Part 1, we only need to verify that, under the assumptions of the proposition,
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\eta/(8\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{5/2})
. By the definition of
\varepsilon_{0}
and
Lemma

D.7
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{0}\leq\|A_{1}-A_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\|K_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|B_{1}-B_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(1+\left\|K_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}})\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}.

Since,
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta/(16\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3})
, this calculation above proves that
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\eta/(8\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{5/2})
.

Part 2

Fix a parameter
\eta_{0}\in(0,1)
to be chosen, and let
\eta
be as in the theorem statement. Switching the roles of the indices
i=1
and
i=2
in the first part of the proposition, we find that if
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta_{0}/(16\left\|P_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3})
, we can establish the following PSD inequalities:

\displaystyle P_{1}\preceq P_{\infty}(K_{2};A_{1},B_{1})\preceq P_{2}+\eta_{0}\|P_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}I.

(C.11)

If in addition
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta/(16\left\|P_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3})
, then we have
\|P_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(1+\eta)\|P_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. Therefore, if
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta_{0}/(16(1+\eta)^{3}\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3})
(replacing
P_{2}
in the denominator by
P_{1}
), then
P_{1}\preceq P_{2}+\eta_{0}(1+\eta)\|P_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}I
.

Selecting
\eta_{0}\leftarrow\eta/(1+\eta)
, we have that if
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta/(16(1+\eta)^{4}\left\|P_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3})
,
P_{1}\preceq P_{2}+\eta\|P_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}I
. Hence, combining with Part
1
, we have that
\|P_{1}-P_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta\|P_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
.
∎

C.5
Proof of Weighted
P
-Perturbation:
Proposition

C.4

Our argument is based on the self-bounding ODE method introduced by
Simchowitz and Foster 2020
, where the perturbation bound is derived from considering an interpolating curve
A(t),B(t)
between the ground-truth instances
(A_{\star},B_{\star})
and estimated instance
(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})
.

Definition C.1

(Interpolating Curves)
.

Given estimates
(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})
of
(A_{\star},B_{\star})
, for
t\in[0,1]
we define,

\displaystyle A(t):=A_{\star}+t(\widehat{A}-A_{\star}),\quad B(t):=B_{\star}+t(\widehat{B}-B_{\star}).

In addition, we define the optimal controller
K(t)
, closed-loop matrix
A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)
, and value function
P(t)
as

\displaystyle K(t):=K_{\infty}(A(t),B(t)),\quad A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t):=A(t)+B(t)K(t),\quad P(t):=P_{\infty}(A(t),B(t)).

Finally, define the following error term in the closed loop matrix

\displaystyle\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\widehat{A}-A_{\star}+(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})K(t).

At the core of the technique is verifying that the instances along the curve
(A(t),B(t))
remain stabilizable, so that the operators
K(t),P(t)
are well defined. The following guarantee, established in
Section

C.5.3
, ensures that this is the case.

Lemma C.8
.

Assume that
(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})
satisfy
2.1
. Then:

1.

The instances
(A(t),B(t))
are stabilizable along
t\in[0,1]
, and
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|P(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}~\leq~1.1\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

2.

\sup_{u,t\in[0,1]}\left\|P_{\infty}(K(t);A(u\cdot t),B(u\cdot t))\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}~\leq~1.2\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

Since instances
(A(t),B(t))
are stabilizable along
t\in[0,1]
,
Lemma

D.18
ensures that
P^{\prime}(t)
is
Frechet differentiable
in the operator norm, and its derivative is

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}P(t)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t),\;E(t)\right),

(C.12)

\displaystyle\text{where }E(t):=A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}P(t)\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)+\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}P(t)A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t).

(C.13)

The formal definition of the Frechet derivative is deferred to
Definition

D.2
; in this section, we shall only use it as a condition to call relevant lemmas. The heart of proposition is based off the following observation which follows from part (c) of
Lemma

D.20
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(P_{\star}-\widehat{P})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}=\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}(P(0)-P(1))\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\leq\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}.

To prove the main result, it remains to show the following bound on the derivative:

\displaystyle\forall t\in[0,1],\quad\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{P}\cdot\varepsilon_{P}\cdot\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(\kappa_{P}\right)}\cdot\phi(\kappa_{P})^{\alpha_{\mathrm{op}}}.

(C.14)

We verify that
Equation

C.14
holds in
Section

C.5.1
.

A key step along the way is the following “change of system” lemma, which allows us to bound the norm of the error in a covariance induced by one closed-loop system
A_{1}+B_{1}K
by that induced by another,
A_{2}+B_{2}K
. This proposition is, in some sense, the mirror image of the change-of-covariance theorems in
Appendix

B
. In those results, we keep the system fixed but change the controller. Here, we keep the controller fixed, but change the system.

Proposition C.9
.

Let
(A_{1},B_{1})
and
(A_{2},B_{2})
be two systems,
K
a controller, and define for
u\in[0,1]
:

\displaystyle\bar{A}(u):=A_{1}+u(A_{2}-A_{1})\quad\bar{B}(u):=B_{1}+u(B_{2}-B_{2}),\quad\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u):=\bar{A}(u)+\bar{B}(u)K.

Assume that
K
stabilizes all instances
(\bar{A}(u),\bar{B}(u))
, so that

\displaystyle\bar{M}:=\max_{u\in[0,1]}\left\|\bar{P}(u)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}<\infty,\quad\text{ where }\bar{P}(u):=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u),\;Q+K^{\mathsf{H}}RK\right).

Finally, define
\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}:=\frac{d}{du}\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)=(A_{2}-A_{1})+(B_{2}-B_{1})K
, let
\Sigma\succeq 0
be a trace class operator, and define the error terms:

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sys},i}^{2}:=\mathrm{tr}\left[\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A_{i}+B_{i}K)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right],\quad i\in\{1,2\}.

Then, setting the operator
\left[X,Y\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}:=\max\{\left\lVert XWY\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}:\|W\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1\}
the following bound holds,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sys},2}^{2}\leq\psi_{\mathrm{diff}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sys},1})\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sys},1}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\text{where }\psi_{\mathrm{diff}}(\varepsilon):=2\exp\left(\frac{3}{2}\bar{M}\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(\frac{e\cdot\left[\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}},\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\bar{M}^{3}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}\right).

Moreover, these relationships hold for any
\varepsilon\geq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sys},1}
.

The proof relies on a careful application of the self-bounding ODE method. The full proof is given in
Section

C.5.2
.

C.5.1
Establishing
Equation

C.14

Proof.

Our proof proceeds in multiple steps. First, we majorize the weighted derivative
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}
in terms of two PSD operators
Y_{1}(t),Y_{2}(t)
; a careful analysis shows it suffices to bound the operator norm of
Y_{1}(t)
and the trace of
Y_{2}(t)
. The term
Y_{1}(t)
is straightforward to control; the term
Y_{2}(t)
requires appeal to the change-of-system error bound in
Proposition

C.9
. The proof concludes with an application of one of our change-of-covariance bounds (
Lemma

B.1
), and some further simplifications.

Majorization by
Y_{1}(t),Y_{2}(t)

Define for all
t\in[0,1]
and
\alpha>0
the quantities:

\displaystyle E_{1}(t):=A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}P(t)A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t),\quad E_{2}(t):=\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}P(t)\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t),\quad E_{[\alpha]}(t):=\frac{\alpha}{2}E_{1}(t)+\frac{1}{2\alpha}E_{2}(t).

By the AM-GM inequality in
Lemma

D.3
,
E(t)\preceq E_{[\alpha]}(t)
for all
t\in[0,1]
and
\alpha>0
(recall that
E(t)
is defined in
Equation

C.13
). Since the solution of the Lyapunov equation preserves PSD order (
Lemma

A.1
),

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t),\;E_{[\alpha]}(t)\right)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}}_{:=-Y_{[\alpha]}(t)}\preceq{\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}}\preceq\underbrace{\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t),\;E_{[\alpha]}(t)\right)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}}_{:=Y_{[\alpha]}(t)}.

Now, observe that since
\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\cdot,\;\cdot\right)
is linear in its second argument, we can express

\displaystyle Y_{[\alpha]}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{2}Y_{1}(t)+\frac{1}{2\alpha}Y_{2}(t),\quad\text{where}

\displaystyle\quad Y_{1}(t):=\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t),\;A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}P(t)A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)\right)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad Y_{2}(t):=\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t),\;\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}P(t)\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\right)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}.

From
Lemma

D.5
, we show that the PSD domination of
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}
by the weighted linear combinations of the form
Y_{[\alpha]}(t)
imply that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}~\leq~\sqrt{\|Y_{1}(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\mathrm{tr}[Y_{2}(t)]}.

(C.15)

We now proceed to bound the terms
\|Y_{1}(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
and
\mathrm{tr}[Y_{2}(t)]
individually. We let
M=1.2\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, which upper bounds
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|P(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
by
Lemma

C.8
.

Bounding
\|Y_{1}(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}

By
Lemma

D.9
we have that
(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}P(t)A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{j}\preceq P(t)(1-\left\|P(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{j}
. Therefore, using our uniform upper bound on
\|P(t)\|
from
Lemma

C.8
, the following inequality holds for
T_{1}
:

\displaystyle\|Y_{1}(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t),\;A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}P(t)A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)\right)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\right)^{j+1}P(t)A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{j+1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}P(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1-\left\|P(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{j+1}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}M^{2}.

(C.16)

Bounding
\mathrm{tr}\left[Y_{2}(t)\right]
via change of system

Commuting traces and rewriting
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{j}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\right)^{j}
as
\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[Y_{2}(t)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t),\;\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}P(t)\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\right)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\left[\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right)\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}P(t)\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle~\leq~M\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right)\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\right].

(C.17)

In the last line, we used that for any
X\succeq 0
, and operators
X,A
,
\mathrm{tr}\left[XA^{\mathsf{H}}YA\right]=\mathrm{tr}\left[AXA^{\mathsf{H}}Y\right]\leq\left\|Y\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\mathrm{tr}\left[AXA^{\mathsf{H}}\right]
.

Note however that our estimation guarantees hold under the true system
A_{\star},B_{\star}
, whereas the above bound holds under a closed loop system involving the matrices
A(t),B(t)
. To this end, we invoke the change of system guarantee,
Proposition

C.9
. We instantiate
Proposition

C.9
with the following substitutions:

•

Take
K\leftarrow K(t)
,
(A_{1},B_{1})\leftarrow(A(t),B(t))
, and
(A_{2},A_{2})\leftarrow(A_{\star},B_{\star})
.

•

By
Lemma

C.8
,
\bar{M}
can be upper bounded by
M:=1.2\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}:=1.2M_{\star}
.

•

\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}=A_{\star}-A(t)+(B_{\star}-B(t))K(t))=-t\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)
. Hence, bounding
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|K(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{M}
:

\displaystyle\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|A_{\star}-A(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|B_{\star}-B(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|K(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{op}}(1+\|K(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}})\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{op}}(1+\sqrt{M})\leq 2\sqrt{M}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{op}}\leq 2.4\sqrt{M_{P_{\star}}}.

(C.18)

•

Define the aligned error,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}:=\max_{t\in[0,1]}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t),\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}.

(C.19)

•

With these substitutions, we can take

\displaystyle\psi(\varepsilon):=2\exp\left(3\cdot 1.2^{3/2}\cdot M_{P_{\star}}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{op}}\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(1.2^{3}eM_{P_{\star}}^{3}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}\right).

(C.20)

Applying these substitutions,
Proposition

C.9
entails

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[Y_{2}(t)\right]

\displaystyle\leq M\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A(t)+B(t)K(t))^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right)\right]\leq M\psi(\varepsilon)\varepsilon,\quad\forall\varepsilon\geq T_{3}

(C.21)

\displaystyle\quad\text{ where }T_{3}:=\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K(t))^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right)\right].

(C.22)

Bounding
T_{3}
via change of controller

Focusing on the remaining trace term
T_{3}
, by
Lemma

B.1
we can switch the controller
K(t)
inside the Lyapunov operator to
K_{0}
and pay a multiplicative constant,

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K(t))^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle~\leq~\mathcal{C}_{K}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right)\right],

(C.23)

where, for
P_{0}=P_{\infty}(K_{0};A_{\star},B_{\star})
and
M_{P_{0}}:=\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{K}:=2\left(1+\frac{64\max_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|K_{0}-K(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}M_{P_{0}}^{3}\log(2M_{P_{0}})^{2}\right).

Next, we recall that
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}=\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)
where
\Sigma_{0}=B_{\star}B_{\star}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}
is equal to the steady-state covariance induced by the initial controller
K_{0}
. By
Lemma

D.10
, we can rewrite the solution to Lyapunov equation as,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right)=\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{0}\right).

By applying
Lemma

D.12
, and recalling that
M_{P_{0}}=\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
,
\left[X,Y\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}:=\max\{\left\lVert XWY\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}:\|W\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1\}
, we can upper bound
\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)\right]
as follows,

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)\right]+(n+1)\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t),\Sigma_{0}\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\|P_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\exp(-\|P_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1}n)

\displaystyle=n\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]+(n+1)\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t),\Sigma_{0}\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}M_{P_{0}}^{3}\exp(-M_{P_{0}}^{-1}n)

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]+(n+1)\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}M_{P_{0}}^{3}\exp(-M_{P_{0}}^{-1}n).

In the last inequality, we used that by
Lemma

D.6
,
\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t),\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}
where
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}
is defined in
Equation

C.19
. Next, by AM-GM and the fact that
\mathrm{tr}\left[XY\right]\leq\mathrm{tr}\left[X^{\prime}Y\right]
for
0\preceq X\preceq X^{\prime}
and
Y\succeq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\left[\left(\widehat{A}-A_{\star}+K(t)(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})\right)^{\mathsf{H}}\left(\widehat{A}-A_{\star}+K(t)(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})\right)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathrm{tr}\left[\left((\widehat{A}-A_{\star})^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})+K(t)^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})K(t)\right)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]

\displaystyle=2\|(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+2\|(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})K(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+2\max_{t\in[0,1]}\|K(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+2M\|(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+2.4M_{P_{\star}}\|\widehat{B}-B_{\star}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}:=\varepsilon_{P}^{2},

(C.24)

where above, we used the fact that
\|K(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\|P(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
by
Lemma

D.7
, which by
Lemma

C.8
is at most
M:=1.2M_{P_{\star}}
for all
t\in[0,1]
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)\right]\leq n\varepsilon_{P}^{2}+(n+1)\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}M_{P_{0}}^{3}\exp(-M_{P_{0}}^{-1}n).

To optimize over
n
, select
n=\lceil M_{P_{0}}\log\frac{M_{P_{0}}^{3}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}\rceil
. Then, going back to
Equation

C.23
and putting things together, we get that:

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{C}_{K}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma_{0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{C}_{K}(2n+1)\varepsilon_{P}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathcal{C}_{K}M_{P_{0}}\varepsilon_{P}^{2}\log_{+}\left(\frac{eM_{P_{0}}^{3}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}\right):=\bar{T}_{3}.

(C.25)

Concluding the proof

Combining
Equations

C.21
,
C.22
and
C.25
, and
M=1.2M_{P_{\star}}
gives

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[0,1]}\mathrm{tr}[Y_{2}(t)]

\displaystyle\leq M\psi(\bar{T}_{3})\bar{T}_{3}=3.6\cdot\mathcal{C}_{K}M_{P_{\star}}M_{P_{0}}\varepsilon_{P}^{2}\log_{+}\left(\frac{eM_{P_{0}}^{3}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}\right)\psi(\bar{T}_{3}).

(C.26)

Now, recall the definition from
Equation

C.20
that

\displaystyle\psi(\varepsilon):=2\exp\left(3\cdot 1.2^{3/2}\cdot M_{P_{\star}}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{op}}\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(1.2^{3}eM_{P_{\star}}^{3}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}\right).

Since this quantity is decreasing in
\varepsilon
, and since
\bar{T}_{3}\geq 3M_{P_{0}}\mathcal{C}_{K}\varepsilon_{P}^{2}\geq 6M_{P_{0}}\varepsilon_{P}^{2}
(recall
\mathcal{C}_{K}~\geq~2
),

\displaystyle\psi(\bar{T}_{3})

\displaystyle\leq\psi(3M_{P_{0}}\mathcal{C}_{K}\varepsilon_{P}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(3\cdot 1.2^{3/2}\cdot M_{P_{\star}}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{op}}\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(1.2^{3}eM_{P_{\star}}^{3}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}}{6M_{P_{0}}\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(4M_{P_{\star}}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(\frac{M_{P_{\star}}^{2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}\right)}\right),

where in the last inequality, we use the simplifications
3\cdot 1.2^{3/2}\leq 4
,
1.2^{3}\cdot e/6\leq 1
, and
M_{P_{\star}}\leq M_{P_{0}}
, since
M_{P_{0}}
is the norm of a suboptimal value function. Hence, continuing from
Equation

C.26
,

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[0,1]}\mathrm{tr}[Y_{2}(t)]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{K}MM_{P_{0}}\varepsilon_{P}^{2}\log_{+}\left(\frac{eM_{P_{0}}^{3}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}\right)\exp\left(4M_{P_{\star}}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(\frac{M_{P_{\star}}^{2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}\right)}\right).

(C.27)

Recall from
Equation

C.24
that
\varepsilon_{P}^{2}:=2\|(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+2.4M_{P_{\star}}\|\widehat{B}-B_{\star}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. A similar computation to that used to derive the bound in
Equation

C.24
(this time, invoking the domination property in
Lemma

D.6
) lets us bound

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}

\displaystyle:=\max_{t\in[0,1]}\left[\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t),\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left[(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{A}-A_{\star}),\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}+2.4M_{P_{\star}}\left[(\widehat{B}-B_{\star})^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{B}-B_{\star}),\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\widehat{A}-A_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]+2.4M_{P_{\star}}\|\widehat{B}-B_{\star}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

Using the elementary inequality
\frac{a+b}{c+b}\leq 1+\frac{a}{c+b}
for
a,b,c\geq 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{algn}}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}\leq 1+\frac{2\|\widehat{A}-A_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]}{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}:=\kappa_{P}.

The above bound on
\mathrm{tr}[Y_{2}(t)]
then simplifies to:

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[0,1]}\mathrm{tr}[Y_{2}(t)]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{K}MM_{P_{0}}\varepsilon_{P}^{2}\log\left(eM_{P_{0}}^{3}\kappa_{P}\right)\exp\left(4M_{P_{\star}}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(M_{P_{\star}}^{2}\kappa_{P}\right)}\right).

(C.28)

Combining with
Equations

C.15
and
C.16
with
M=1.2M_{P_{\star}}
yields

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\sqrt{\|Y_{1}(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\mathrm{tr}[Y_{2}(t)]}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{M_{P_{0}}M_{P_{\star}}^{3}\mathcal{C}_{K}\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\varepsilon_{P}^{2}\log\left(eM_{P_{0}}^{3}\kappa_{P}\right)}\exp\left(2M_{P_{\star}}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(M_{P_{\star}}^{2}\kappa_{P}\right)}\right).

Simplifying the bound

Recall that
M=1.2\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, and
M_{P_{\star}}:=\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. Hence,
M_{P_{0}}\geq M_{P_{\star}}
, since
P_{0}\succeq P_{\star}
, as
P_{\star}
is the optimal value function for the pair
(A_{\star},B_{\star})
. Hence, from
Lemma

D.7
.
M_{P_{0}}\geq M_{P_{\star}}\geq 1
,
\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\|P_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}:=M_{P_{0}}
, and
\left\|K(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq 1.1\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1.1M_{P_{\star}}
. We note also that

\displaystyle\left\|K_{0}-K(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq 2\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}+2\left\|K(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq 2(M_{P_{0}}^{2}+M^{2})=2(M_{P_{0}}^{2}+1.2^{2}M_{P_{\star}}^{2}),

which is less than or equal to
9M_{P_{0}}^{2}
. Noting
M_{P_{0}}\geq 1
, we can bound

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{K}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+\frac{M_{P_{0}}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}M_{P_{0}}^{3}\log(2M_{P_{0}})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim M_{P_{0}}^{5}\log(2M_{P_{0}})^{2}\left(1+\frac{\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}\right).

Therefore, we get that
\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}
is upper bounded by:

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{M_{P_{0}}^{6}M_{P_{\star}}^{3}\left(1+\smash{\tfrac{\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}}\right)\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log\left(eM_{P_{0}}^{3}\kappa_{P}\right)}\cdot\log(2M_{P_{0}})\cdot\exp\left(2M_{\star}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(M_{P_{\star}}^{2}\kappa_{P}\right)}\right)\cdot\varepsilon_{P}.

To conclude, we bound

\displaystyle\log(2M_{P_{0}})\cdot\sqrt{\log\left(eM_{P_{0}}^{3}\kappa_{P}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\log(2M_{P_{0}})\left(\sqrt{\log\left(e\kappa_{P}\right)}+\sqrt{\log(M_{P_{0}}^{3})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim M_{P_{0}}\sqrt{\log_{+}(\kappa_{P})}.

Therefore, the bound further simplifies to:

\displaystyle\lesssim M_{P_{0}}^{4}M_{P_{\star}}^{3/2}\sqrt{\left(1+\smash{\tfrac{\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}}\right)\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log_{+}\left(\kappa_{P}\right)}\exp\left(2M_{\star}^{5/2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{\log_{+}(\kappa_{P})}\right)\cdot\varepsilon_{P}

\displaystyle\lesssim M_{P_{0}}^{4}M_{P_{\star}}^{3/2}\sqrt{\left(1+\smash{\tfrac{\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}}}\right)\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log_{+}\left(\kappa_{P}\right)}\cdot\phi(\kappa_{P})^{2M_{P_{\star}}^{5/2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}}\cdot\varepsilon_{P},

where
\phi(z)=\exp(\sqrt{\log z})
.

Further simplifications in finite dimensions

To conclude, we remark on how
\kappa_{P}
can be replaced by
1+\mathrm{cond}(\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0})
, where
\mathrm{cond}(\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0})
denotes the condition number, in finite dimensions. To see this, we note that the term
\left\|\widehat{A}-A_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]
in
\kappa_{P}
arose from an upper bound on
\left[(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{A}-A_{\star}),\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}
. By
Lemma

D.6
, one can similarly bound
\left[(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{A}-A_{\star}),\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\leq\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert\widehat{A}-A_{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}
. In finite dimensions with invertible
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}
, we can therefore compute:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widehat{A}-A_{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\leq\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0})^{-1}\left\lVert(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}{2\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0})}.

Thus we can replace:

\displaystyle\log(1+\frac{2\left[(\widehat{A}-A_{\star})^{\mathsf{H}}(\widehat{A}-A_{\star}),\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}}{\varepsilon_{P}^{2}})\leq\log(1+\frac{2\left\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\varepsilon_{P}^{2}}{2\varepsilon_{P}^{2}\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0})})=\log(1+\mathrm{cond}(\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0})).

∎

C.5.2
Proof of
Proposition

C.9

Before beginning the proof, let us recall the setup. We let
(A_{1},B_{1})
and
(A_{2},B_{2})
be two systems, and
K
a controller, and define for
u\in[0,1]

\displaystyle\bar{A}(u):=A_{1}+u(A_{2}-A_{1})\quad\bar{B}(u):=B_{1}+u(B_{2}-B_{1}),\quad\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u):=A(u)+B(u)K.

Assume that
K
stabilizes all instances
(\bar{A}(u),\bar{B}(u))
, so that

\displaystyle\bar{M}:=\max_{u\in[0,1]}\left\|\bar{P}(u)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}<\infty,\quad\text{ where }\bar{P}(u):=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u),\;Q+K^{\mathsf{H}}RK\right).

Finally, define
\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}:=\frac{d}{du}\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)=(A_{2}-A_{1})+(B_{2}-B_{1})K
.

Proof.

The proof is based on analyzing the behavior of the curve,

\displaystyle z(u):[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}:=\mathrm{tr}\left[\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right].

Since
\bar{M}<\infty
,
\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)
is stable for all
u\in[0,1]
, and hence the Lyapunov operator is well defined implying that
z(u)
is finite.

In order to simplify our presentation, we let
Z(u)=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)
. Since
\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)
is a linear curve supported on stable operators,
Lemma

D.19
ensures that
Z(u)
is continuously Frechet-differentiable, with Frechet derivative equal to

Z^{\prime}(u)=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}Z(u)\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}}+\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)Z(u)\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\right).

(C.29)

From part (a) of
Lemma

D.20
,
z(u)
is continuously differentiable (as a real-valued curve), and its derivative is
z^{\prime}(u)=\mathrm{tr}[\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}},Z^{\prime}(u)]
. By applying the PSD AM-GM inequality twice (
Lemma

D.3
), we have that for any
\alpha>0
,

Z^{\prime}(u)=\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}Z(u)\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}}+\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)Z(u)\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\preceq\frac{1}{2}\alpha\cdot\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}Z(u)\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{-1}\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)Z(u)\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}}.

(C.30)

Combining
Equation

C.30
and
Equation

C.29
, and optimizing over
\alpha
, we can upper bound the derivative of
z(u)
,

\displaystyle z^{\prime}(u)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\left[\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}Z(u)\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}}+\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)Z(u)\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle~\leq~\left(\underbrace{\mathrm{tr}\left[\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}Z(u)\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\right)\right]}_{:=R_{1}}\right)^{1/2}

(C.31)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\underbrace{\mathrm{tr}\left[\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)Z(u)\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}}\right)\right]}_{:=R_{2}}\right)^{1/2}.

(C.32)

We now bound each of
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
individually.

Bounding
R_{1}

Beginning with
R_{1}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}Z(u)\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad~\leq~\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\mathrm{tr}\left[\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}Z(u)\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad~\leq~\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\cdot\left\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u),\;I\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\mathrm{tr}\left[\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}Z(u)\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad~\leq~\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\bar{P}(u)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\mathrm{tr}\left[\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}Z(u)\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\right].

\displaystyle\quad=\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\bar{P}(u)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot z(u)

\displaystyle\quad=\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bar{M}\cdot z(u)

In the second line, we have used
Lemma

D.8
. Furthermore, the second to last line is justified by the following observation. Since
I\preceq Q
and
I\preceq R
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u),\;I\right)\preceq\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u),\;Q+K^{\mathsf{H}}RK\right)=\bar{P}(u).

Bounding
R_{2}

We first notice that,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)Z(u)\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{j+1}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)(A_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\mathsf{H}})^{j+1}

\displaystyle=\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)\cdot\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right)\cdot\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right)-\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)-\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)

\displaystyle\preceq\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right).

The third line the calculation above follows from definition of the solution to the Lyapunov equation, i.e
Equation

A.4
. The second to last line is justified by
Lemma

D.10
. Setting
X:=\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\succeq 0
,

\displaystyle R_{2}=\mathrm{tr}\left[X\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)Z(u)\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}}\right)\right]\leq\mathrm{tr}\left[X\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right].

Recall
\left[X,Y\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}:=\max\{\left\lVert XWY\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}:\|W\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1\}
. By
Lemma

D.12
, the following bound holds for any
n~\geq~0
:

\displaystyle R_{2}\leq\mathrm{tr}\left[X\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[X\mathsf{dlyap}\left(\bar{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}(u)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+(n+1)\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\left\|\bar{P}(u)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\exp(-\left\|\bar{P}(u)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1}n)

\displaystyle=n\cdot z(u)

\displaystyle\qquad+(n+1)\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\left\|\bar{P}(u)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\exp(-\left\|\bar{P}(u)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1}n)

\displaystyle~\leq~n\cdot z(u)+(n+1)\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\bar{M}^{3}\exp(-\bar{M}^{-1}n).

Concluding the proof

Combining our bounds for
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
, then for any
n\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle z^{\prime}(u)

\displaystyle~\leq~\sqrt{\left(\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bar{M}\cdot z(u)\right)\left(n\cdot z(u)+(n+1)\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\bar{M}^{3}\exp(-\bar{M}^{-1}n)\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{~\leq~}\sqrt{n\bar{M}}\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}z(u)+\sqrt{n\bar{M}}\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left(z(u)^{1/2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{n+1}{n}\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\bar{M}^{3}\exp(-\bar{M}^{-1}n)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{~\leq~}\sqrt{n\bar{M}}\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}z(u)+\sqrt{n\bar{M}}\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left(\frac{z(u)}{2}+\frac{n+1}{2n}\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\bar{M}^{3}\exp(-\bar{M}^{-1}n)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{n\bar{M}}\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}_{:=a}\cdot z(u)+\underbrace{\sqrt{n\bar{M}}\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\bar{M}^{3}\exp(-\bar{M}^{-1}n)}_{:=b},

where
(i)
uses concavity of the square-root, and
(ii)
applies AM-GM.
In other words, the scalar function
z(u)
exhibits the self-bounding property
z^{\prime}(u)\leq az(u)+b
for
a,b
defined above. Hence, for any slack parameter
\eta>0
,
4
4

4

This is useful to ensure strict domination of one ODE by another
a standard ODE comparison inequality (see, e.g.
Simchowitz and Foster 2020
, Lemma D.1) implies that
z(u)\leq\tilde{z}(u)
, where
\tilde{z}(u)
solves the analogous ODE with equality:

\displaystyle\tilde{z}(u)=a\tilde{z}^{\prime}(u)+b+\eta,\quad\tilde{z}(0)=z(0)+\eta.

(C.33)

The differential equation above has solution:

\displaystyle\tilde{z}(u)~\leq~\left(z(0)+\eta+\frac{b+\eta}{a}\right)\exp(a\cdot u)-\frac{b+\eta}{a}.

Taking
\eta\to 0
and bounding
z(u)\leq\tilde{z}(u)
yields

\displaystyle z(1)~\leq~z(0)\exp(a)+\frac{b}{a}\exp(a).

Substituting in the relevant quantities, the following bound holds for any
n\geq 1
:

\displaystyle z(1)\leq\exp\left(\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{n\bar{M}}\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)\left(z(0)+\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\bar{M}^{3}\exp(-\bar{M}^{-1}n)\right),

Taking
n=\lceil\bar{M}\log\left(\frac{\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\bar{M}^{3}}{z(0)}\right)\rceil\leq\bar{M}\log_{+}\left(\frac{e\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\bar{M}^{3}}{z(0)}\right)
lets us bound

\displaystyle\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\bar{M}^{3}\exp(-\bar{M}^{-1}n)\leq z(0),

and hence

\displaystyle z(1)\leq 2\exp\left(\frac{3}{2}\bar{M}\left\|\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt{\log_{+}\left(\frac{e\cdot\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\bar{M}^{3}}{z(0)}\right)}\right)z(0).

Recalling
X=\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\mathsf{H}}\bar{\Delta}_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}
, and recognizing
z(1)
as
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sys},2}
and
z(0)
as
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sys},1}
, the bound follows. Note also that this bound on the derivative is necessarily non-decreasing in the
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sys},2}
argument.
∎

C.5.3
Proof of
Lemma

C.8

Proof.

We apply the first bullet point of
Proposition

C.3
to establish both parts of the lemma.

Part 1:

Take
(A_{1},B_{1})=(A_{\star},B_{\star})
, and
(A_{2},B_{2})=(A(t),B(t))
. Since
(A_{2},B_{2})
lies on the segment joining
(A_{\star},B_{\star})
and
(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})
, we have

\displaystyle\max\{\|A_{1}-A_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|B_{1}-B_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}\leq\max\{\|A_{\star}-\widehat{A}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|B_{\star}-\widehat{B}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}

Hence, if
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{\eta}{16\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}}
for
\eta=1/11
,
Proposition

C.3
implies
\left\|P(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(1+\eta)\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1.1\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
.

Part 2

For part
2
, take
(A_{1},B_{1})=(A(t),B(t))
and
(A_{2},B_{2})=(A(u\cdot t),B(u\cdot t))
. Again, it holds that
\max\{\|A_{1}-A_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|B_{1}-B_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}
. Then, if
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta/(16\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3})
, where
P_{1}=P_{\infty}(A_{1},B_{1})
,
Proposition

C.3
implies that

\displaystyle\left\|P_{\infty}(K(t);A(u\cdot t),B(u\cdot t))\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(1+\eta)\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

From part
1
of the present lemma,
\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(1+\eta)\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. Hence, if
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{\eta}{16(1+\eta)^{3}\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}}
, we have
\left\|P_{\infty}(K(t);A(u\cdot t),B(u\cdot t))\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(1+\eta)^{2}\left\|P_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. Computing
(1+\eta)^{2}\leq 1.2
, and noting that
16(1+\eta)^{3}/\eta\leq 229
concludes the proof.

∎

Appendix D
Technical Lemmas

This section states and prove the main technical tools used throughout the paper.
Section

D.1
contains linear algebraic tools,
Section

D.2
gives tools for controlling terms involving Lyapunov operators,
Section

D.3
states and proves a comparison theorem between the eigendecay of a PSD operator
\Lambda
and its image
\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A,\;\Lambda\right)
. Finally,
Section

D.4
addresses the relevant differentiability considerations that arise in infinite dimensional spaces.

D.1
Linear Algebra

Lemma D.1
.

Let
Z\succeq 0
and
Y_{1},\dots,Y_{T}
be bounded linear operators on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\right)Z\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}\preceq 2\sum_{t=1}^{T}t^{2}\,Y_{t}ZY_{t}^{\mathsf{H}}.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{x}
be any vector in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
, then

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\langle\mathbf{x},\;\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\right)Z\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{x}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\langle Z^{1/2}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{x},\;Z^{1/2}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{x}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\lVert\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z^{1/2}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}^{2}.

Therefore it suffices to show that, for any
\mathbf{x}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{x}_{T}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
,
\left\lVert\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}^{2}\leq 2\sum_{t=1}^{T}t^{2}\cdot\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}^{2}
. We argue by Cauchy Schwartz,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}^{2}=\left\lVert\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{t}\cdot t\cdot\mathbf{x}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\right)\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\lVert t\cdot\mathbf{x}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}^{2}.

Since
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{t^{2}}<\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\leq 2
, the bound follows.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Let M be a positive, semi-definite linear operator, then

\log\det(I+M)~\leq~\mathrm{tr}\left[M\right]

Proof.

If
M
has eigenvalues
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
, then
I+M
has eigenvalues
\{1+\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
. Since the determinant of a linear operator is equal to the product of its eigenvalues, we have that,

\log\det(I+M)=\sum_{i}\log(1+\sigma_{i})~\leq~\sum_{i}\sigma_{i}=\mathrm{tr}\left[M\right],

where we have used the numerical inequality
\log(1+x)~\leq~x
for all
x~\geq~0
.
∎

Lemma D.3
.

Let
X,Y,P:\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
be linear operators and let
P
be positive semi-definite, then for any
\alpha>0
we have that

XPY^{\mathsf{H}}\preceq\frac{\alpha}{2}XPX^{\mathsf{H}}+\frac{1}{2\alpha}YPY^{\mathsf{H}}.

Proof.

Letting
\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
, the proof follows by direct application of Cauchy-Schwarz and the AM-GM inequality:

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{v},\;XPY^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}
