\displaystyle=\left\langle\sqrt{\alpha}P^{\frac{1}{2}}X^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v},\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}}P^{\frac{1}{2}}Y^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\lVert\sqrt{\alpha}P^{\frac{1}{2}}X^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}\left\lVert\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}}P^{\frac{1}{2}}Y^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\frac{\alpha}{2}\left\lVert P^{\frac{1}{2}}X^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\alpha}\left\lVert P^{\frac{1}{2}}Y^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{2}\left\langle\mathbf{v},\;XPX^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}+\frac{1}{2\alpha}\left\langle\mathbf{v},\;YPY^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}.

∎

Lemma D.4
.

Let
X:\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
be a self-adjoint operator and let
Y\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}
be a trace class, positive semi-definite operator. If,
-Y\preceq X\preceq Y
then
\|X\|_{\mathsf{tr}}~\leq~\mathrm{tr}\left[Y\right]
.

Proof.

Let
X=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}\mathbf{q}_{j}\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}
denote the spectral decomposition of
X
. Then,

\displaystyle\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 1}|\lambda_{j}|=\sum_{j\geq 1}|\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}X\mathbf{q}_{j}|,

since
-Y\preceq X\preceq Y
,
|\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}X\mathbf{q}_{j}|\leq\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}Y\mathbf{q}_{j}
for each
j
. Thus,
\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}Y\mathbf{q}_{j}=\mathrm{tr}\left[Y\right]
, since the elements
\mathbf{q}_{j}
form an orthonormal basis.
∎

Lemma D.5
.

Let
X,Y_{1},Y_{2}
be symmetric operators with
Y_{1},Y_{2}\succeq 0
. For all
\alpha>0
, define
Y_{[\alpha]}:=\frac{\alpha}{2}Y_{1}+\frac{1}{2\alpha}Y_{2}
, and suppose that
-Y_{[\alpha]}\preceq X\preceq Y_{[\alpha]}
for any
\alpha>0
. Then,

\displaystyle\|X\|_{\mathsf{HS}}\leq\sqrt{\|Y_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\mathrm{tr}\left[Y_{2}\right]}.

Proof.

Let
X=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}\mathbf{q}_{j}\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}
denote the spectral decomposition of
X
. Then,

\displaystyle\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 1}\lambda_{j}^{2}=\sum_{j\geq 1}(\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}X\mathbf{q}_{j})^{2}.

For any fixed
j
, we have
|\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}X\mathbf{q}_{j}|\leq\inf_{\alpha>0}\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\top}Y_{[\alpha]}\mathbf{q}
, since for all
\alpha
,
Y_{[\alpha]}\preceq X\preceq Y_{[\alpha]}
. Moreover, we compute

\displaystyle|\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}X\mathbf{q}_{j}|\leq\inf_{\alpha>0}\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}Y_{[\alpha]}\mathbf{q}_{j},

\displaystyle=\inf_{\alpha>0}\frac{\alpha}{2}\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}Y_{1}\mathbf{q}_{j}+\frac{1}{2\alpha}\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}Y_{2}\mathbf{q}_{j}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}Y_{1}\mathbf{q}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}Y_{2}\mathbf{q}_{j}}\leq\sqrt{\|Y_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}\sqrt{\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}Y_{2}\mathbf{q}_{j}},

where we note that
\min_{\alpha>0}\frac{a}{2\alpha}+\frac{b}{2\alpha}=\sqrt{ab}
for nonegative
a,b\geq 0
. Combining the above two displays,

\displaystyle\|X\|_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left(\sqrt{\|Y_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}\sqrt{\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}Y_{2}\mathbf{q}_{j}},\right)^{2}=\left\|Y_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}Y_{2}\mathbf{q}_{j}.

Since
\mathbf{q}_{j}
are an orthonormal basis,
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{q}_{j}^{\mathsf{H}}Y_{2}\mathbf{q}_{j}=\mathrm{tr}\left[Y_{2}\right]
. The bound follows.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

Define the operator
\left[X,Y\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}:=\max\{\left\lVert XWY\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}:\|W\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1\}
. Then for any
X^{\prime}\succeq X\succeq 0
,
\left[X,Y\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\leq\left[X^{\prime},Y\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}
. Similarly, for
Y^{\prime}\succeq Y\succeq 0
, then
\left[X,Y^{\prime}\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\leq\left[X,Y\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}
.

Proof.

Let us prove the first point; the second is analogous.
Fix
\varepsilon>0
, and let
W
be such that
\left\lVert XWY\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}\geq\left[X,Y\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}-\varepsilon
. Let
WY=U\Sigma V^{\mathsf{H}}
denote the singular value decomposition of
WY
. Then,
\left\lVert XWY\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}=\left\lVert XU\Sigma V^{\mathsf{H}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}=\left\lVert XU\Sigma U^{\mathsf{H}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}
, since
VU^{\mathsf{H}}
is orthonormal, and thus conjugating by it does not alter the trace norm. Since
X^{\prime}\succeq X\succeq 0
and
U\Sigma U^{\mathsf{H}}\succeq 0
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert XU\Sigma U^{\mathsf{H}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\left[XU\Sigma U^{\mathsf{H}}\right]\leq\mathrm{tr}\left[X^{\prime}U\Sigma U^{\mathsf{H}}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\lVert X^{\prime}U\Sigma U^{\mathsf{H}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}=\left\lVert X^{\prime}U\Sigma V^{\mathsf{H}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}=\left\lVert X^{\prime}WY\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}\leq\left[X^{\prime},Y\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}.

The bound follows.
∎

D.2
Lyapunov Theory

Lemma D.7
.

Let
(A_{1},B_{1})
be a stabilizable instance with stabilizing controller
K_{1}
, and let
P_{1}=P_{\infty}(K_{1};A_{1},B_{1})
be the associated value function. Then, if
R\succeq I
and
Q\succeq I
, it holds that
P_{1}\succeq I
and
\left\|K_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}
. In particular,
P_{\infty}(A_{1},B_{1})\succeq I
, and
\left\|K_{\infty}(A_{1},B_{1})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq{\left\|P_{\infty}(A_{1},B_{1})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}^{1/2}
.

Proof.

We have the identity:

\displaystyle P_{1}=P_{\infty}(K_{1};A_{1},B_{1})=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{1}+B_{1}K_{1},\;Q+K_{1}^{\top}RK_{1}\right)\succeq Q+K_{1}RK_{1}

Thus,
P_{1}\succeq Q\succeq I
, and since
R\succeq I
,
K_{1}^{\top}K_{1}\preceq K_{1}^{\top}RK_{1}\preceq P_{1}
, so that
\left\|K_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\left\|P_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}
.
∎

Lemma D.8
.

Let
Y
be a trace class operator, and suppose
\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X^{\mathsf{H}},\;I\right)
is bounded. Then,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X,\;Y\right)\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}~\leq~\left\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X^{\mathsf{H}},\;I\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert Y\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}.

In particular, if
Y\succeq 0
is PSD, then

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}[\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X,\;Y\right)]~\leq~\left\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X^{\mathsf{H}},\;I\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\mathrm{tr}[Y].

Proof.

We write out
Y
in its spectral decomposition,
Y=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}v_{i}\otimes v_{i}\cdot\lambda_{i}
, and use the form of the Lyapunov solution to get that

\displaystyle\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X,\;Y\right)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(X^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}YX^{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(X^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}(v_{i}\otimes v_{i})X^{j}\cdot\lambda_{i}

\displaystyle\preceq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(X^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}(v_{i}\otimes v_{i})X^{j}\cdot|\lambda_{i}|:=Z.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X,\;Y\right)\succeq-\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(X^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}(v_{i}\otimes v_{i})X^{j}\cdot|\lambda_{i}|=-Z.

Therefore, from
Lemma

D.4

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X,\;Y\right)\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{tr}[Z]=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X,\;v_{i}\otimes v_{i}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}\cdot|\lambda_{i}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}|\left\langle v_{i},\;\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X^{\mathsf{H}},\;I\right)v_{i}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}|\cdot|\lambda_{i}|

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X^{\mathsf{H}},\;I\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}|\lambda_{i}|.

Noting that
\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\lambda_{i}=\left\lVert Y\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}
finishes the first part of the proof. For the second part, notice that if
Y\succeq 0
, then the nuclear and trace norms norms coincide.

∎

Lemma D.9
.

Let
A
be stable and
P=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A,\;\Sigma\right)
be the corresponding value function for some
\Sigma\succeq I
, then for all integers
j~\geq~0
,

\displaystyle(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}PA^{j}\preceq P(1-\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{j}.

In particular, for all
j\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|A^{j}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}(1-\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{j}.

Hence, for any over
\Sigma^{\prime}\succeq 0
,
\|\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A,\;\Sigma\right)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}
.

Proof.

Since
P
satisfies the Lyapunov equation,
A^{\mathsf{H}}PA-P+\Sigma=0
, we can write for any
x\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
:

\displaystyle\left\langle x,\;A^{\mathsf{H}}PAx\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle x,\;Px\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}-\left\langle x,\;\Sigma x\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle x,\;Px\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}\left(1-\frac{\left\langle x,\;\Sigma x\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}}{\left\langle x,\;Px\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}}\right)

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\langle x,\;Px\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}(1-\frac{1}{\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}).

In the last line, we have use the assumption that
\Sigma\succeq I
. Hence,
A^{\mathsf{H}}PA\preceq P(1-\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})
where
\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>1
. Repeating this argument, we can in fact show that,

\displaystyle(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}PA^{j}\preceq P(1-\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{j}.

For the second guarantee, since
\Sigma\succeq I
, we have
P\succeq\Sigma\succeq I
. Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|A^{j}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}=\left\|(A^{j})^{\mathsf{H}}A^{j}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}~\leq~\left\|(A^{j})^{\mathsf{H}}PA^{j}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}~\leq~\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}(1-\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{j}.

∎

For the next lemma, we recall the higher order Lyapunov operator from
Definition

A.2
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dlyap}_{(m)}\left(A,\;\Lambda\right):=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}\Lambda A^{j}(j+1)^{m}.

Lemma D.10
.

Let
A
be a stable linear operator and
\Sigma
be self-adjoint, the the following identity holds:

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}A^{j}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}A^{j}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}\cdot(j+1).

Equivalently,

\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right)=\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right).

Proof.

To simplify the proof, let
\Gamma=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)
.
Since
\Gamma=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)
is the solution to the Lyapunov equation
A\Gamma A^{\mathsf{H}}+\Sigma-\Gamma=0
, we have that

A\Gamma A^{\mathsf{H}}\Sigma=\Gamma-\Sigma.

By repeating this argument, we can in fact show that:

(A)^{j}\Gamma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}=\Gamma-\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}A^{i}\Gamma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{i}.

Using the fact that
\Gamma=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}A^{j}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}
, it follows that
(A)^{j}\Gamma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}=\sum_{i=j}^{\infty}A^{i}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{i}
. Therefore, we can rewrite
\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right)
as follows,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}A^{j}\Gamma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{i=j}^{\infty}A^{i}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}A^{j}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}\cdot(j+1).

which is exactly
\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)
.
∎

Lemma D.11
.

Let
A
be stable and
P=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A,\;Q+K^{\mathsf{H}}RK\right)
be the corresponding value function, then for all integers
n~\geq~0
,

For
m=1:

\displaystyle\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\preceq n\cdot\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)+(n+1)\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\exp(-\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1}n)\cdot I

For
m=2:

\displaystyle\mathsf{dlyap}_{(2)}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\preceq n^{2}\cdot\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)+(n^{2}+2n+2)\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{4}\exp(-\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1}n)\cdot I

Proof.

We begin by expanding out the definition of
\mathsf{dlyap}_{(m)}\left(A(t)^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dlyap}_{(m)}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}A^{j}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}\cdot(j+1)^{m}

\displaystyle\preceq n^{m}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}A^{j}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}+\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}A^{j}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}\cdot(j+1)^{m}

\displaystyle\preceq n^{m}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)+\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}A^{j}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}\cdot(j+1)^{m},

(D.1)

where we have let the sum go to infinity in the first term.
Focusing on the second term,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}A^{j}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}\cdot(j+1)^{m}

\displaystyle\preceq I\cdot\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}\left\|A^{j}(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot(j+1)^{m}

\displaystyle=I\cdot\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}\left\|(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}A^{j}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot(j+1)^{m}

(
\left\|NN^{\mathsf{H}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\left\|N^{\mathsf{H}}N\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
)

\displaystyle\preceq I\cdot\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}\left\|(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}PA^{j}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot(j+1)^{m}

(
P\succeq I
)

\displaystyle\preceq\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}(j+1)\cdot(1-\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{j}\cdot I.

(
Lemma

D.9
)

The result then follows by applying the following two identities regarding geometric series, which hold for
c\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}(1-c)^{j}\cdot(j+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-c)^{n}(cn+1)}{c^{2}}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}(1-c)^{j}\cdot(j+1)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-c)^{n}(c^{2}n^{2}+2cn-c)}{c^{3}},

and using the fact that
(1-c)^{t}~\leq~\exp(-ct)
.
∎

Lemma D.12
.

Let
A
be stable and
P=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A,\;Q+K^{\mathsf{H}}RK\right)
be the corresponding value function, then for all integers
n~\geq~0
, and all PSD, bounded operators
X
, and
\Sigma\succeq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[X\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right]\leq n\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[X\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right]+(n+1)\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\exp(-\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1}n),

where
\left[X,Y\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}:=\max\{\left\lVert XWY\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}:\|W\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1\}
.

Proof.

From
Equation

D.1
and the fact that
\mathrm{tr}\left[WY\right]\leq\mathrm{tr}\left[WZ\right]
for
W\succeq 0
and
Y\preceq Z
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[X\mathsf{dlyap}_{(1)}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq n\mathrm{tr}\left[X\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Sigma\right)\right]+\mathrm{tr}\left[X\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}A^{j}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}\cdot(j+1)\right].

We can then bound,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[XA^{j}\Sigma(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}\right]\leq\|A^{j}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\lVert X\cdot\frac{A^{j}}{\|A^{j}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}\cdot\Sigma\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}\leq\|A^{j}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}}=\|A^{j}(A^{j})^{\mathsf{H}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left[X,\Sigma\right]_{\mathrm{algn}},

(D.2)

where we note the above bound holds even if
A^{j}=0
. The bound now follows from the computation given in the proof of
Lemma

D.11
.
∎

D.3
Spectrum Comparison under Lyapunov Operator

Lemma D.13

(Iterated Weyl’s Eigenvalue Inequality)
.

Let
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
be a sequence of PSD operators, and let
\lambda_{j}(\cdot)
denote the
j
-eigenvalue. Then,
\lambda_{j}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\lceil j/n\rceil}(X_{i})
.

Proof.

Consider
n=2
. Then, for any indices
k_{1},k_{2}
such that
k_{1}+k_{2}-1\leq j
,
\lambda_{i}(X_{1}+X_{2})\leq\lambda_{k_{1}}(X_{1})+\lambda_{k_{2}}(X_{2})
. Hence, for general
n
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right)\leq\lambda_{k_{1:n-1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}X_{i}\right)+\lambda_{k_{n}}(X_{n}),

where
k_{n}+k_{1:n-1}\leq j+1
. Iterating,

\displaystyle\lambda_{k_{1:n-1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}X_{i}\right)\leq\lambda_{k_{n-1}}\left(X_{n-1}\right)+\lambda_{k_{1:n-2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-2}X_{i}\right),

where
k_{n-1}+k_{1:n-2}\leq k_{1:n-1}+1
. Continuing, we have that for any
k_{1},\dots,k_{n}
with
\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\leq j+(n-1)
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{k_{i}}\left(X_{i}\right).

Taking
k_{i}=\lceil j/n\rceil
, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}=n\lceil j/n\rceil
. Now, if
j/n
is integral, then
n\lceil j/n\rceil=j
. Otherwise,
\lceil j/n\rceil=\lceil(j-1)/n\rceil\leq 1+\frac{j-1}{n}
, so
n\lceil j/n\rceil\leq n+j-1
. In either case,
\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\leq j+(n-1)
, as needed.
∎

Lemma D.14
.

Let
X
be a bounded operator on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
, and
\Lambda\succeq 0
be a PSD operator on
\mathcal{H}
. Then
\sigma_{j}(X\Lambda X^{\mathsf{H}})\leq\|X\|^{2}_{\mathrm{op}}\sigma_{j}(\Lambda)
.

Proof.

Since
\sigma_{j}(X\Lambda X^{\mathsf{H}})=\sigma_{j}(X\Lambda^{1/2})^{2}
, it suffices to show that for any two bounded operators
A,B
,
\sigma_{j}(AB)\leq\|A\|\sigma_{j}(B)
. This follows using the variation representation of singular values:

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}(AB)

\displaystyle=\min_{\text{ subspaces }\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{H}\text{ of dimension }j}\left(\max_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{V}:\|\mathbf{q}\|_{\mathcal{H}}=1}\|AB\mathbf{q}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\min_{\text{ subspaces }\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{H}\text{ of dimension }j}\left(\max_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{V}:\|\mathbf{q}\|_{\mathcal{H}}=1}\|B\mathbf{q}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\right)

\displaystyle=\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sigma_{j}(B).

∎

Lemma D.15
.

Let
A
be a stable operator,
\Lambda\succeq 0
, and let
\Sigma=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A^{\mathsf{H}},\;\Lambda\right)
, and
P=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A,\;I\right)
. Then, for all indices
j,n\geq 1
, we can bound

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}(\Sigma)

\displaystyle\leq\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left(\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(\Lambda)+(1-\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{n}\left\|\Lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq k}\sigma_{j}(\Sigma)

\displaystyle\leq(n+1)\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left(\sum_{j\geq\lceil\frac{k}{n+1}\rceil}\sigma_{j}(\Lambda)+(1-\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{n}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Lambda\right]\right).

Moreover, from monotonicity of
\mathsf{dlyap}
(
Lemma

A.1
), the above also holds for
P=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A,\;Z\right)
for any
Z\succeq I
. In particular, if
n\geq\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log\frac{2\left\|\Lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\lambda}
, then,

\displaystyle d_{\lambda}(\Sigma)\leq(n+1)d_{\lambda/(2\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2})}(\Lambda).

Proof.

Fix an integer
n
, and define the matrices

\displaystyle X_{i}:=A^{i}\Lambda(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{i},\quad Y_{n}:=\sum_{i>n}X_{i}.

By
Lemma

D.13
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}(\Sigma)=\sigma_{j}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}+Y_{n})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(X_{i})+\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(Y_{n}).

(D.3)

Now, for any index
k
, we can bound

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}(X_{i})=\sigma_{k}(A^{i}\Lambda(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{i})=\sigma_{k}(A^{i}\Lambda^{1/2})\leq\|A^{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\sigma_{k}(\Lambda).

Hence,

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}(\Sigma)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|A^{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\right)\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(\Lambda)+\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(Y_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(\Lambda)+\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(Y_{n}),

(D.4)

where in the last line we use
Lemma

D.9
to bound
\|A^{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}
by a geometric series.

Now, let us bound the operator norm and trace of
Y_{n}
. Again, by
Lemma

D.9
, we have

\displaystyle\|Y_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i>n}A^{i}\Lambda(A^{\mathsf{H}})^{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sum_{i>n}\|A^{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\Lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}(1-\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{n}\left\|\Lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

(D.5)

and similarly

\displaystyle\|Y_{n}\|_{\mathsf{tr}}

\displaystyle\leq\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}(1-\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{n}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Lambda\right].

(D.6)

Combining these bounds, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}(\Sigma)

\displaystyle\leq\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(\Lambda)+\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(Y_{n}),

(by
Equation

D.4
)

\displaystyle\leq\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(\Lambda)+\left\|Y_{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}},

(by
Equation

D.5
)

\displaystyle\leq\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left(\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(\Lambda)+(1-\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{n}\left\|\Lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right).

and,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq k}\sigma_{j}(\Sigma)

\displaystyle\leq\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\sum_{j\geq k}\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(\Lambda)+\sum_{j\geq k}\sigma_{\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil}(Y_{n})

(by
Equation

D.4
)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}(n+1)\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\sum_{j\geq\lceil\frac{k}{n+1}\rceil}\sigma_{j}(\Lambda)+(n+1)\sum_{j\geq\lceil\frac{k}{n+1}\rceil}\sigma_{j}(Y_{n})

(D.7)

\displaystyle\leq(n+1)\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\sum_{j\geq\lceil\frac{k}{n+1}\rceil}\sigma_{j}(\Lambda)+(n+1)\mathrm{tr}\left[Y_{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(n+1)\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left(\sum_{j\geq\lceil\frac{k}{n+1}\rceil}\sigma_{j}(\Lambda)+(1-\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{n}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Lambda\right]\right),

(by
Equation

D.6
)

concluding the proof. Here, in inequality
(i)
, we used that summing over the indices
\lceil\frac{j}{n+1}\rceil{j}
for
j\geq k
sums over only indices
j^{\prime}\geq\lceil\frac{k}{n+1}\rceil
, and includes each such index at most
n
times.

To see why the last statement holds, for
a:=(n+1)d_{\lambda/(2\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}})}
,
by the first statement,

\displaystyle\sigma_{a}(\Sigma)~\leq~\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left(\sigma_{d_{\lambda/(2\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}})}}(\Lambda)+\exp(-n\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})\left\|\Lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right).

By definition of
d_{\lambda}
,
\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\sigma_{d_{\lambda/(2\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}})}}(\Lambda)~\leq~\lambda/2
and for
n~\geq~\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log(2\mathrm{tr}\left[\Lambda\right].\lambda)
the second term is also smaller than
\lambda/2
. Hence
d_{\lambda}(\Sigma)
is at most
(n+1)d_{\lambda/(2\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2})}(\Lambda)
.

∎

D.4
Frechet Differentiability

Definition D.1
.

Let
\mathscr{B}_{\mathrm{op}}
denote the Banach space consisting of all operator-norm bounded operators on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
, and let
\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{op}}
denote the subspace of self-adjoint bounded operators on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
.

Lemma D.16
.

Let
X\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathrm{op}}
have spectral radius
\rho(X)<1
, and for
Y\in\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{op}}
, define
\mathcal{T}_{X}[Y]=Y-X^{\mathsf{H}}YX
. Then,
\mathcal{T}_{X}[\cdot]
is a bounded linear operator on
\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{op}}
, and has an inverse which is also bounded. Hence, the solution to
\mathcal{T}_{X}[Y]=Q
is bounded for any
Q\in\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{op}}
, and given by
\mathsf{dlyap}\left(X,\;Q\right)
.

Definition D.2

(Frechet Derivative)
.

Let
\mathscr{B}_{1},\mathscr{B}_{2}
be a banach space with norms
\|\cdot\|_{\mathscr{B}_{1}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\mathscr{B}_{2}}
. Given a subset
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathscr{B}_{1}
, we say
f:\mathcal{U}\to\mathscr{B}_{2}
is
Frechet Differentiable
at
x\in\mathscr{B}_{1}
if there exists linear mapping
\mathrm{D}f(x):\mathscr{B}_{1}\to\mathscr{B}_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\|h\|_{\mathscr{B}_{1}}\to 0}\frac{\|f(x+h)-\mathrm{D}f(x)[h])\|_{\mathscr{B}_{2}}}{\|h\|}=0.

We say that
f
is
Frechet continuously differentriable
at
x
if
f
is Frechet differentiable in an open neighborhood about
x
, and
x\mapsto\mathrm{D}f(x)[h]
is a continuous in the dual norm
\|g\|_{\mathscr{B}_{1}^{*}}:=\sup_{\|h\|_{\mathscr{B}}=1}g(h)
.

An important case is where
f(t):\mathbb{R}\to\mathscr{B}
is a curve. In this case, the Frechet Derivative exists if there exists an
f^{\prime}(t)
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{\|f(t+\varepsilon)-f^{\prime}(t)\|_{\mathscr{B}}}{\varepsilon}=0.

Lemma D.17

(Implicit Function Theorem
[
Whittlesey 1965
]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{B}_{1},\mathscr{B}_{2}
be a two Banach spaces, and let
f:\mathscr{B}_{1}\times\mathscr{B}_{2}\to\mathscr{B}_{2}
be a continuously Frechet differentiable function at
(x_{0},y_{0})\in\mathscr{B}_{1}\times\mathscr{B}_{2}
. Suppose that the mapping
h_{2}\mapsto\mathrm{D}f(x_{0},y_{0})[0,h_{2}]
is a Banach space isopmorphism on
\mathscr{B}_{2}
(i.e, it admits a a bounded linear inverse). Then, there exists a neighborhood
\mathcal{U}
around
x_{0}
and
\mathcal{V}
around
y_{0}
and a Frechet-differentiable function
g:\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{V}
on which
g(x)
is the unique solution to
f(x,g(x))=0
.

Lemma D.18
.

Let
(A_{1},B_{1})
and
(A_{2},B_{2})
be two instance, and define the curve
A(t)=A_{1}+t(B_{2}-B_{1})
and
B(t)=B_{1}+t(B_{2}-B_{1})
. If
(A(t),B(t))
is stabilizable for all
t\in[0,1]
,

•

P(t):=P_{\infty}(A(t),B(t))
is Frechet differentiable on
[0,1]
in the operator norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{op}}
.

•

The Frechet derivative of
P(t)
is given by

\displaystyle P^{\prime}(t)=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t),\;E(t)\right)\text{ where }E(t):=A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}P(t)\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)+\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t)^{\mathsf{H}}P(t)A_{\mathrm{cl}}(t),

(D.8)

where
\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t):=(A_{2}-A_{1})+(B_{2}-B_{1})K_{\infty}\left(A(t),B(t)\right)
.

Proof.

The result is the infinite-dimensional analogue of Lemma 3.1 in
Simchowitz and Foster 2020
. Define the function
\mathcal{F}(t,P):=\mathbb{R}\times\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{op}}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(t,P):=A(t)^{\mathsf{H}}PA(t)-P+Q-A(t)^{\mathsf{H}}PB(t)(R+B(t)^{\mathsf{H}}PB(t))^{-1}B(t)^{\mathsf{H}}PA(t)

Then,
P(t)
solves
\mathcal{F}(t,P)=0
. One can verify that
\mathcal{F}(t,P)
is a Frechet continuously differentiable function
5
5

5

Indeed, the first term in a polynomial in bounded operators, and the second term is a product of surge polynomials, and the matrix inverse
(R+B(t)^{\mathsf{H}}PB(t))^{-1}
. Since
R+B(t)^{\mathsf{H}}PB(t)\succeq R\succ 0
is finite dimensional, one can express perturbations of
(R+B(t)^{\mathsf{H}}PB(t))^{-1}
as a convergence series, which can be used to verify continuous differentiability.
The computation of Lemma 3.1 in
Simchowitz and Foster 2020
(carried out now in infinite dimensions)
shows that,

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{F}(t,P)\cdot(\updelta t)+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}P}\mathcal{F}(t,P)\cdot(\updelta P)\right\}\big|_{P=P(t)}=\mathcal{T}_{A(t)+B(t)K(t)}[\updelta P]+E(t)\cdot\updelta t,

where
K(t)=K_{\infty}(A(t),B(t))
, and where
E(t)
is defined in
Equation

D.8
, and
\mathcal{T}_{X}[Y]=Y-X^{\mathsf{H}}YX
. Since
(A(t),B(t))
is stabilizable,
A(t)+B(t)K(t)
is stable and thus
\mathcal{T}_{A(t)+B(t)K(t)}[\cdot]
is a bounded linear operator on
\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{op}}
with bounded inverse. It follows from the
Lemma

D.17
that
P(t)
admits a Frechet Derivative
P^{\prime}(t)
, and its derivative must
\mathcal{T}_{A(t)+B(t)K(t)}[\updelta P]+E(t)=0
. Hence, by
Lemma

D.16
,
P^{\prime}(t)
is given by
Equation

D.8
.
∎

Lemma D.19
.

Let
A(t)=A_{1}+(A_{2}-A_{1})t
be a curve on
[0,1]
with
A(t)\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathrm{op}}
stabilizable. Set
\Delta=A_{2}-A_{1}
.Then,
t\mapsto\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A(t),\;\Sigma\right)
is Frechet differentiable in the operator norm, and

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A(t),\;\Sigma\right)=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A(t),\;N\right)

(D.9)

where
N:=\Delta^{\mathsf{H}}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A(t),\;\Sigma\right)A(t)+A(t)^{\mathsf{H}}\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A(t),\;\Sigma\right)\Delta
.

Proof.

Set
P(t):=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A(t),\;\Sigma\right)
. Recall the operator
\mathcal{T}_{X}[Y]=Y-X^{\mathsf{H}}YX
. Then,
P(t)
solves
\mathcal{F}(t,P)=0
, where
\mathcal{F}(t,P)=\mathcal{T}_{(A(t)}[P]-\Sigma
. It is straightforward to check that
\mathcal{F}(t,P)
is Frechet continuously differentiable. A direct computation reveals

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{F}(t,P)\cdot(\updelta t)+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}P}\mathcal{F}(t,P)\cdot(\updelta P)\right\}\big|_{P=P(t)}=\mathcal{T}_{A(t)}[\updelta P]+N(t)\cdot\updelta t,

where
N(t)
is defined in the lemma. Moreover, since
A(t)
is stabilizable,
\mathcal{T}_{A(t)}
is a bounded linear operator with bounded inverse (
Lemma

D.16
). Hence, the implicit function theorem (
Lemma

D.17
) shows that
P(t)
admits a Frechet Derivative
P^{\prime}(t)
, and its derivative must satisfy
\mathcal{T}_{A(t)+B(t)K(t)}[\updelta P]+N(t)=0
. Consequently, by
Lemma

D.16
,
P^{\prime}(t)
is given by
Equation

D.8
.
∎

Lemma D.20

(Traces of Derivatives on
\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{op}}
)
.

Let
P(t):[0,1]\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{op}}
be a continuously Frechet-differentiable curve. Moreover, let
\Gamma
be a fixed trace-class operator on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
. Then,

(a)

The function
f(t)=\mathrm{tr}\left[\Gamma P(t)\right]
is a continously differentiable function, with derivative
f^{\prime}(t)=\mathrm{tr}\left[\Gamma P^{\prime}(t)\right]
.

(b)

If
\Gamma
is also PSD,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Gamma^{1/2}(P(1)-P(0))\Gamma^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}\leq\max_{t\in[0,1]}\left\lVert\Gamma^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Gamma^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}.

(c)

Similarly, if
\Gamma
is PSD,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Gamma^{1/2}(P(1)-P(0))\Gamma^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\leq\max_{t\in[0,1]}\left\lVert\Gamma^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Gamma^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}.

Proof.

For any trace-class
\Gamma
, the mapping
P\mapsto\mathrm{tr}\left[\Gamma P\right]
is a bounded linear functional on the space
\mathscr{S}_{\mathrm{op}}
. Hence, the map commutes with Frechet derivatives, and thus part (a) follows.

To prove part (b), write
\left\lVert\Gamma^{1/2}(P(1)-P(0))\Gamma^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}=\max_{X:\|X\|=1}\mathrm{tr}(X\Gamma^{1/2}(P(1)-P(0)\Gamma^{1/2})=\mathrm{tr}(\tilde{\Gamma}(P(1)-P(0)))
, where
\tilde{\Gamma}=\Gamma^{1/2}X\Gamma^{1/2}
. One can check that
\tilde{\Gamma}
is also trace class (see, e.g.
Dragomir 2014
) and hence
f(t)=\mathrm{tr}\left[\tilde{\Gamma}P(t)\right]
is a continuously differentiable function by the first part of the lemma, with derivative
f^{\prime}(t)=\mathrm{tr}\left[\tilde{\Gamma}P^{\prime}(t)\right]
. Thus, from the mean-value theorem,
\mathrm{tr}(\tilde{\Gamma}(P(1)-P(0)))=f^{\prime}(t)=\mathrm{tr}\left[\tilde{\Gamma}P^{\prime}(t)\right]
for some
t\in[0,1]
. Thus,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Gamma^{1/2}(P(1)-P(0))\Gamma^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}

\displaystyle=\max_{X:\|X\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Gamma^{1/2}X\Gamma^{1/2}(P(1)-P(0))\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{X:\|X\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1}\max_{t\in[0,1]}\mathrm{tr}\left[\Gamma^{1/2}X\Gamma^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\right].

Swapping the maxima and rearranging the trace, we see that the resulting term is just

\displaystyle\max_{X:\|X\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1}\max_{t\in[0,1]}\mathrm{tr}\left[X\Gamma^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Gamma^{1/2}\right]=\max_{t\in[0,1]}\left\lVert\Gamma^{1/2}P^{\prime}(t)\Gamma^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{tr}}.

The proof of part (c) is nearly identical, except that the constraint on the variational parameter
X
is strengthed to
\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\leq 1
.
∎

Part II
Estimation Rates

Appendix E
Estimating System Operators

In this section, we prove estimation rates for the system operators
A_{\star},B_{\star}
estimated via ridge regression on data collected under a single trajectory. In particular, throughout this section, as per our definition of the dynamics in
Equation

1.1
, we assume that data
\{(\mathbf{x}_{t},\mathbf{u}_{t},)\}_{t=1}^{T+1}
is generated according to,

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}_{t+1}=A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\mathbf{x}_{t}+B_{\star}\mathbf{u}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t},

where
\mathbf{u}_{t}=K_{0}\mathbf{x}_{t}+\mathbf{v}_{t}
. Here,
K_{0}
is a stabilizing controller,
\mathbf{v}_{t}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}I)
, and
\mathbf{w}_{T}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}})
. As before, we let
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}:=\Sigma_{\star}(K_{0},\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}})=\lim_{t\rightarrow}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}]=\mathsf{dlyap}\left((A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0})^{\mathsf{H}},\;B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right)
be the stationary state covariance when inputs are chosen according to the exploratory policy above. We define
A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}:=A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0}
and estimate
A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}},B_{\star}
via two separate regressions:

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}

\displaystyle:=\argmin_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{x}_{t+1}-A_{\mathrm{cl}}\mathbf{x}_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\left\lVert A_{\mathrm{cl}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

(E.1)

\displaystyle\widehat{B}

\displaystyle:=\argmin_{B}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{x}_{t+1}-B\mathbf{v}_{t}\|^{2}.

(E.2)

The following lemma provides closed form expressions for the estimation error.

Lemma E.1
.

Let
\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}
and
\widehat{B}
be defined as in
Equation

E.1
and
Equation

E.2
, then

\displaystyle A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}-\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}

\displaystyle=\lambda A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}-\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle B_{\star}-\widehat{B}

\displaystyle=-\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\mathbf{x}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{v}_{t}\right)\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{t}\right)^{-1}.

Proof.

For the first statement, by taking the first order optimality conditions for the optimization problem in (
E.1
), we have that,

\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}=\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}.

Subtracting out the following quantity from both sides,

A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}

and multiplying by
-1
we get the first identity. The second follows directly from computing the optimality conditions for (
E.2
).
∎

E.1
Estimating
B_{\star}

We start by presenting the estimation guarantees for
B_{\star}
since they are significantly easier to prove than those for
A_{\star}
. This is because the covariates
\mathbf{v}_{t}
are independent and finite dimensional.

Proposition E.2

(B estimation)
.

Let
T\gtrsim\max\left\{d_{u}\log\left(d_{u}\right),\;d_{u}\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right\}
then with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert B_{\star}-\widehat{B}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{d_{u}\left(\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]+\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\log\left(\frac{d_{u}}{\delta}\right)\right)}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}T}.

Proof.

Using
Lemma

E.1
we know that the error in estimating
B_{\star}
has the following form, which we can upper bound as follows,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\mathbf{x}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{v}_{t}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{t}\right)^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle~\leq~\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{t}\right)^{2}}\left\lVert\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\mathbf{x}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{v}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}.

Upper bounding Hilbert-Schmidt norm

Focusing on the Hilbert-Schmidt norm term, we let
\mathbf{e}_{1}\dots\mathbf{e}_{d_{u}}
be the standard basis for
\mathbb{R}^{d_{u}}
. Then, we can expand the Hilbert Schmidt norm as,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\mathbf{x}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{v}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{u}}\underbrace{\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\mathbf{x}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\left\langle\mathbf{v}_{t},\mathbf{e}_{i}\right\rangle\|^{2}}_{:=E_{i}}.

(E.3)

Now, we deal with each
E_{i}
individually. To do so, we notice that
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\mathbf{x}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\left\langle\mathbf{v}_{t},\mathbf{e}_{i}\right\rangle
is a mean zero gaussian vector in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
with covariance operator equal to,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j,k=1}^{T}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\mathbf{w}_{j}\right)\otimes\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\mathbf{x}_{k}+\mathbf{w}_{k}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}\left\langle\mathbf{v}_{j},\mathbf{e}_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{e}_{i}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}]A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{\mathsf{H}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right)\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}

\displaystyle\preceq\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}T\cdot\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}.

The first equality follows from the fact that for
j\neq k
,
\left\langle\mathbf{v}_{j},\mathbf{e}_{i}\right\rangle
and
\left\langle\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{e}_{i}\right\rangle
are independent and both mean zero. Therefore, only the diagonal terms remain and by definition of
\mathbf{v}_{t}
,
\mathbb{E}\left\langle\mathbf{v}_{t},\mathbf{e}_{i}\right\rangle^{2}=\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}
for all
i
and
t
. The upper bound in the second line is a consequence of the fact that, by definition of the dynamics and
\mathsf{dlyap}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right]\preceq\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}
. Moreover, since
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}
is the solution to a Lyapunov equation,

\displaystyle A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{\mathsf{H}}=\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}-\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}-\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}},

the
\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}
cancel out. Now, by applying the Hanson-Wright inequality (
Lemma

E.8
), with probability
1-\delta_{i}
,
E_{i}
is upper bounded by,

2T\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]+5T\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\log(1/\delta_{i})\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}.

Letting
\delta_{i}=\frac{1}{2d_{u}}\delta
, we get that with probability,
1-\delta/2
, the expression in (
E.3
) is less than or equal to

5d_{u}T\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\left(\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]+\log\left(\frac{2d_{u}}{\delta}\right)\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\right).

Lower bounding minimum eigenvalue

To finish off the proof, we lower bound the minimum eigenvalue of
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{t}
. Recall that
\mathbf{v}_{t}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}I)
for all
t
. Hence, by
Lemma

E.9
(and our lower bound on
T
), with probability
1-\delta/2
,

\lambda_{\min}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{v}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{i}\right)^{2}~\geq~(9/1600)^{2}T^{2}\sigma_{\mathbf{u}}^{4}.

Here, we have used that
M
, as defined in
Lemma

E.9
, is upper bounded by
\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}d_{u}
and that
\sigma_{\min}(\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}I)=\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}
.

Wrapping up

Putting everything together, with probability
1-\delta
, for
T
larger than the stated threshold,

\left\lVert B_{\star}-\widehat{B}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{d_{u}\left(\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]+\log\left(\frac{d_{u}}{\delta}\right)\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\right)}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}T}.

∎

E.2
Estimating
A_{\star}

For this subsection, we define the following the following quantities to simplify our notation,

\displaystyle\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}

\displaystyle V_{\lambda}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}:\left\langle\mathbf{q},\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\mathbf{q}\right\rangle~\geq~\lambda\right\}

\displaystyle t_{0}

\displaystyle:=c_{0}\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log_{+}\left(\lambda^{-1}\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right),

where
P_{0}:=P_{\infty}(K_{0};A_{\star},B_{\star})
and
c_{0}
is a universal constant.

Let
S_{\lambda}
denote the orthogonal projection operator onto the subspace
V_{\lambda}
. Similarly, let
\overline{S}_{\lambda}
denote the projection operator onto the orthogonal complement of
V_{\lambda}
. Recall our definition of
\Sigma_{0}:=B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}
. Lastly, we will make extensive reference to
d_{\lambda}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{tail},\lambda}
as defined in
Theorem

3.1
. In particular, if we let
(\sigma_{j})_{j=1}^{\infty}=(\sigma_{j}(\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}))_{j=1}^{\infty}
be the eigenvalues of
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}
then,

\displaystyle\textstyle d_{\lambda}:=|\{\sigma_{j}:\sigma_{j}~\geq~\lambda\}|,\quad\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{tail},\lambda}:=\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{j>d_{\lambda}}\sigma_{j}.

Lastly, recall
A_{\star}:=A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{0}
.

Proposition E.3

(A estimation)
.

Let
T
be such that

T\gtrsim\max\left\{d_{\lambda}\log_{+}\left(\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]\lambda^{-1}\right),\;d_{\lambda}\log_{+}\left(1/\delta\right)\right\}+\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log_{+}\left(\lambda^{-1}\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right).

Then, with probability
1-\delta
,

\left\lVert(\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\lesssim\lambda\left\lVert A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+\frac{W_{\mathrm{tr}}}{T}(d_{\lambda}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{tail},\lambda})\log_{+}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]}{\delta\lambda}\right).

Furthermore, the above guarantee holds for any
\overline{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}
equal to

\overline{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}:=\argmin_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}\in\mathcal{B}}\left\langle(A_{\mathrm{cl}}-\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}),(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}+\lambda I)(A_{\mathrm{cl}}-\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}})\right\rangle,

where
\mathcal{B}
is a compact and convex set containing
A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}
.

Proof.

Using the identity from
Lemma

E.1
,

\displaystyle(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}-\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}})\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\lambda A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}.

Taking the Hilbert-Schmidt norm of both sides, we get that

\left\lVert(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}-\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}})\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

is less than or equal to,

2\underbrace{\left\lVert\lambda A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}}_{N_{1}}+2\underbrace{\left\lVert\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}}_{N_{2}}.

Since projections onto convex sets are non-expansive, incorporating a projection step as in the definition of
\overline{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}
, doesn’t change the above guarantee since

\left\lVert(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}-\overline{A_{\mathrm{cl}}})\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}+\lambda I\right)^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}~\leq~\left\lVert(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}-\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}})\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}+\lambda I\right)^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}.

Bounding the bias

Next, we bound each of these terms separately. For the first, we have that,

N_{1}~\leq~\lambda\left\lVert A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}.

Bounding the noise

For the second term, we apply
Lemma

E.4
, to get that with probability
1-\delta/2
,

\displaystyle N_{2}

\displaystyle~\leq~\frac{1}{T}\left\lVert\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{T}W_{\mathrm{tr}}\cdot\left(d_{\lambda}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{tail},\lambda}\right)\log_{+}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]}{\delta\lambda}\right).

Therefore, with probability
1-\delta/2
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}})(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}+\lambda I)^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\lesssim\lambda\left\lVert A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}+\frac{1}{T}W_{\mathrm{tr}}\cdot\left(d_{\lambda}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{tail},\lambda}\right)\log_{+}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]}{\delta\lambda}\right).

(E.4)

Now, since we have chosen
T
to be large enough, we apply
Proposition

E.6
to get that with probability
1-\delta/2

\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\preceq c\cdot(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}+\lambda I)

where
c
is a universal constant. This finishes the proof since,

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\left[(\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}})\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}(\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}})\right]

\displaystyle~\leq~c\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[(\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}})(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}+\lambda I)(\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}})\right]

\displaystyle=c\cdot\left\lVert(\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}})(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}+\lambda I)^{1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}.

The final result then follows by applying a union bound and combining this last inequality with (
E.4
).
∎

Lemma E.4
.

The following inequality holds with probability
1-\delta
,

\left\lVert\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda T\cdot I\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\lesssim W_{\mathrm{tr}}\cdot\log_{+}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]}{\delta\lambda}\right)\left(d_{\lambda}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{tail},\lambda}\right)

Proof.

Define
\mathbf{z}_{t}:=B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}
. Note that
\mathbf{z}_{t}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{0})
, where
\Sigma_{0}
is again defined as,

\Sigma_{0}=B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}.

Now, let
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{q}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{q}_{j}\cdot\sigma_{j}
be the eigendecomposition of this linear operator
\Sigma_{0}
where the
\mathbf{q}_{j}
form an orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
. By definition of the Hilbert-Schmidt norm,

\left\lVert\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda T\cdot I\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\underbrace{\|\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda T\cdot I\right)^{-1/2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\left\langle\mathbf{z}_{t},\mathbf{q}_{j}\right\rangle\|^{2}}_{:=E_{j}}.

(E.5)

Due to our choice of basis, we have that
\left\langle\mathbf{z}_{t},\mathbf{q}_{j}\right\rangle
is a zero-mean sub-Gaussian random variable with sub-Gaussian parameter
\sigma_{j}
. In particular, since the
\mathbf{z}_{t}
are drawn i.i.d from
\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{0})
, we have that for all
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{E}\exp(\gamma\left\langle\mathbf{q}_{j},\mathbf{z}_{t}\right\rangle)=\exp\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\left\langle\Sigma_{0}\mathbf{q}_{j},\mathbf{q}_{j}\right\rangle\right)=\exp\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\sigma_{j}\right).

Self-normalized inequality

Using this decompostion, we can bound each
E_{j}
via a self-normalized martingale bound for vectors in Hilbert space. In particular by
Lemma

E.10
, with probability
1-\delta_{j}
,

\displaystyle E_{j}

\displaystyle~\leq~2\sigma_{j}\log\left(\frac{\det\left(I+\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)^{1/2}}{\delta_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{j}\log\det\left(I+\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)+2\sigma_{j}\log(1/\delta_{j}).

Setting
\delta_{j}=\frac{3}{\pi^{2}}j^{-2}\cdot\delta
, with probability
1-\delta_{j}
,
E_{j}
has the following upper bound,

\displaystyle E_{j}

\displaystyle~\leq~\sigma_{j}\log\det\left(I+\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)+2\sigma_{j}\log\left(\frac{\pi^{2}}{3\delta}\right)+4\sigma_{j}\log(j).

Using a union bound and summing up over all
j
, we get that with probability
1-\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\delta_{j}=1-\delta/2
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}+\lambda T\cdot I\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left(\log\det\left(I+\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)+2\log\left(\frac{\pi^{2}}{3\delta}\right)\right)\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{j}

\displaystyle+4\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{j}\log(j).

Bounding log determinant & simplifying

Lastly, we can apply
Lemma

E.5
, to conclude that with probability
1-\delta/2
,

\log\det\left(I+\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\lesssim d_{\lambda}\log\left(1+\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]\frac{2}{\delta\lambda}\right)+\lambda^{-1}\log_{+}(1/\delta)\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right].

Now using the fact that
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{j}=\mathrm{tr}\left[B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right]=\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{0}\right]
, the target quantity is less than or equal to a universal constant times:

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{0}\right]\left(d_{\lambda}\log\left(1+\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]\frac{2}{\delta\lambda}\right)+\lambda^{-1}\log_{+}(1/\delta)\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right]+\log\left(\frac{\pi^{2}}{3\delta}\right)\right)+W_{\mathrm{tr}}.

Furthermore, by definition of
W_{\mathrm{tr}}
,
\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{0}\right]~\leq~W_{\mathrm{tr}}
. A short calculation shows this above satisfies,

\displaystyle\lesssim W_{\mathrm{tr}}\cdot\log_{+}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]}{\delta\lambda}\right)\left(d_{\lambda}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{tail},\lambda}\right).

∎

Lemma E.5
.

With probability
1-\delta
,

\log\left(\det\left(I+\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\right)~\leq~d_{\lambda}\log\left(1+\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]\frac{2}{\delta\lambda}\right)+7\lambda^{-1}\log_{+}(2/\delta)\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right].

Proof.

Let
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{q}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{q}_{i}\cdot\sigma_{i}
be the eigendecomposition of
\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}
and recall the following definitions.

\displaystyle V_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\{\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}:\left\langle\mathbf{z},\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\mathbf{z}\right\rangle~\geq~\lambda\}

\displaystyle d_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dim}(V_{\lambda}).

Partitioning the spectrum

We recall that for any self-adjoint, PSD linear operator
M
,
\det(M)
is equal to the product
\Pi_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{i}
of the eigenvalues
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
of
M
. Therefore,
\log\det(M)
is equal to
\sum\log(\sigma_{i})
and
\log\det(I+M)=\sum\log(1+\sigma_{i})
. Using this identity, we now bound our target quantity as,

\displaystyle\log\left(\det\left(I+\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\log(1+\sigma_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i:\mathbf{q}_{i}\in V_{\lambda}}\log(1+\sigma_{i})+\sum_{i:\mathbf{q}_{i}\notin V_{\lambda}}\log(1+\sigma_{i})

\displaystyle~\leq~d_{\lambda}\log\left(1+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{i}\right)+\sum_{i:\mathbf{q}_{i}\notin V_{\lambda}}\sigma_{i}

\displaystyle=d_{\lambda}\log\left(1+\mathrm{tr}\left[\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right]\right)+\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right].

To go from the second to the third line, we used the inequality
\log(1+x)~\leq~x
for all
x~\geq~0
and the fact that
\dim(V_{\lambda})=d_{\lambda}
. In the last line, we have used definition of
\overline{S}_{\lambda}
as a projection operator. In further detail,

\displaystyle\sum_{i:\mathbf{q}_{i}\in V_{\lambda}}\log(1+\sigma_{i})~\leq~d_{\lambda}\log(1+\sigma_{1})~\leq~d_{\lambda}\log\left(1+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{i}\right).

Bounding the top

Now, by Markov’s inequality, with probability
1-\delta/2
,

\mathrm{tr}\left[\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right]~\leq~\frac{2\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}]}{\lambda\delta\cdot T}~\leq~\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]\frac{2}{\delta\lambda}.

Bounding the tail

Define
\tilde{\mathbf{x}}
as,

\tilde{\mathbf{x}}=\begin{bmatrix}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\mathbf{x}_{1}\\
\overline{S}_{\lambda}\mathbf{x}_{2}\\
\dots\\
\end{bmatrix}.

Then,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right]=\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\overline{S}_{\lambda}\mathbf{x}_{t}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{\lambda T}\|\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\|^{2},

where the last norm is taken in the relevant Hilbert space. Now, we notice that
\tilde{\mathbf{x}}
is a zero mean Gaussian. Furthermore, the trace norm of its covariance operator can be upper bounded as follows,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathrm{tr}\left[\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\right]=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right]\right]\preceq T\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right].

Therefore, we can apply the Hanson-Wright inequality (
Lemma

E.8
) to conclude that with probability
1-\delta/2
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right]

\displaystyle~\leq~2\lambda^{-1}\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right]+5\lambda^{-1}\log(2/\delta)\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right]

\displaystyle~\leq~7\lambda^{-1}\log_{+}(2/\delta)\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right].

Finishing the proof

In conclusion, by combining the previous parts, we get that with probability
1-\delta
,

\log\left(\det\left(I+\frac{1}{\lambda T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\right)~\leq~d_{\lambda}\log\left(1+\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]\frac{2}{\delta\lambda}\right)+7\lambda^{-1}\log_{+}(2/\delta)\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\overline{S}_{\lambda}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\right].

∎

Proposition E.6
.

For
t_{0}
and
d_{\lambda}
defined as in introduction to
Section

E.2
, if

T-t_{0}\gtrsim\max\left\{d_{\lambda}\log_{+}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]}{\lambda}\right),\;d_{\lambda}\log_{+}\left(1/\delta\right)\right\}

then with probability
1-\delta
,

\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\preceq c\cdot\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}+\lambda I\right)

where
c
is a universal constant.

Proof.

Recall our definition of
V_{\lambda}
as the subspace of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
corresponding to the directions where the state covariance
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}
has large eigenvalues,

V_{\lambda}=\left\{\mathbf{q}:\left\langle\mathbf{q},\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\mathbf{q}\right\rangle~\geq~\lambda\right\}.

Furthermore, we review the definitions of
S_{\lambda}
, the orthogonal projection operator onto the subspace
V_{\lambda}
, and
\overline{S}_{\lambda}
, the orthogonal projection operator on the complement of
V_{\lambda}
. Since
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}
is a trace class operator,
V_{\lambda}
is a finite dimensional subspace. Therefore,
d_{\lambda}=\mathsf{dim}(V_{\lambda})<\infty
.

For any vector
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
, we have that,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{z},\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\mathbf{z}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~\leq~2\left\langle S_{\lambda}\mathbf{z},\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}S_{\lambda}\mathbf{z}\right\rangle+2\left\langle\overline{S}_{\lambda}\mathbf{z},\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\overline{S}_{\lambda}\mathbf{z}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~\leq~2\left\langle S_{\lambda}\mathbf{z},\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}S_{\lambda}\mathbf{z}\right\rangle+2\lambda.

In order to complete the proof, it suffices to show that,

S_{\lambda}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}S_{\lambda}\preceq c\cdot S_{\lambda}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}S_{\lambda}.

In short, we have reduced the proof to showing a PSD upper bound for finite dimensional operators. By
Lemma

E.7
, for
t~\geq~t_{0}
, we have that
S_{\lambda}\mathbf{x}_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{t})
for
\Sigma_{t}\succeq\frac{\lambda}{2}I
. Therefore, for
T-t_{0}
greater than the lower bound in the statement of the proposition, we can apply
Lemma

E.9
, to conclude that with probability
1-\delta
,

S_{\lambda}\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}^{1/2}S_{\lambda}\preceq c\cdot S_{\lambda}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}S_{\lambda}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma E.7
.

For
t~\geq~t_{0}
,
S_{\lambda}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right]S_{\lambda}\succeq\frac{\lambda}{2}I
, where
I\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\lambda}\times d_{\lambda}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{v}\in V_{\lambda}
be a unit vector. Then, by definition of
V_{\lambda}
,
\left\langle\mathbf{v},\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle~\geq~\lambda
. Furthermore, by properties of the dynamical system,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{t-2}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\left(B_{\star}B_{\star}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right)\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}.

Before moving on, we recall the form of the steady-state covariance operator
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}
,

\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}=\mathsf{dlyap}\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{\mathsf{H}},\;B_{\star}B_{\star}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\left(B_{\star}B_{\star}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right)\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}.

By the previous two equations, we have that:

\left\langle\mathbf{v},\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbb{E}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle=\left\langle\mathbf{v},\sum_{j=t-1}^{\infty}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\left(B_{\star}B_{\star}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right)\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle.

Therefore for any
\mathbf{v}\in V_{\lambda}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbb{E}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbf{v},\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathbf{v},\sum_{j=t-1}^{\infty}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\left(B_{\star}B_{\star}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right)\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~\geq~\lambda-\left\langle\mathbf{v},\sum_{j=t-1}^{\infty}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\left(B_{\star}B_{\star}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right)\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle.

To finish the proof, we show that for any
\mathbf{v}\in V_{\lambda}
, the second term is no smaller than
-\lambda/2
. To do so, we proceed as in
Lemma

D.11
. Recall that
B_{\star}B_{\star}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}=\Sigma_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{v},\sum_{j=t-1}^{\infty}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\left(B_{\star}B_{\star}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right)\left(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\right)^{\mathsf{H}}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\|B_{\star}B_{\star}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{j=t-1}^{\infty}\left\|A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{j=t-1}^{\infty}\left\|(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\|\Sigma_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{j=t-1}^{\infty}\left\|(A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{\mathsf{H}})^{j}P_{0}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{j}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\|\Sigma_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{j=t-1}^{\infty}(1-\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{j}

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}(1-\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})^{t-1}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\|\Sigma_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{(t-1)}{\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}\right).

The fourth inequality follows from applying
Lemma

D.9
. For
t~\geq~1+\lceil\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log\left(\frac{2}{\lambda}\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)\rceil
, the expression above is smaller than
\frac{\lambda}{2}
. Lastly, we note that since
\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}>1
,

t_{0}:=3\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log_{+}\left(\frac{2}{\lambda}\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)~\geq~1+\left\lceil\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log\left(\frac{2}{\lambda}\left\|P_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)\right\rceil.

∎

E.3
Formal Statement and Proof of
Proposition

3.1

Proposition

3.1
.

Assume that
3
holds, and define
\Delta_{r}:=\frac{1}{16}\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{stable}}^{2}-s_{r+1}^{2}
,
\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}:=c\frac{\Delta_{r}}{\rho}
, where
\rho,r
are defined as in
3
and
c
is a universal constant. Then, for
T~\geq~T_{\mathsf{init}}
, and
(A_{0},B_{0})
computed as in the
\mathsf{WarmStart}
algorithm, with probability
1-\delta

\max\left\{\left\|A_{0}-A_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}},\left\|B_{0}-B_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\}~\leq~\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{stable}}.

Here,
P_{\mathsf{init}}=P_{\infty}(K_{\mathsf{init}};A_{\star},B_{\star})
,
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}:=\Sigma_{\star}(K_{\mathsf{init}},\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}})
, and
T_{\mathsf{init}}
is equal to a universal constant times the maximum of the following three quantities,

1.

d_{\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}}\log_{+}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}\right]}{\delta\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}}\right)+\left\|P_{\mathsf{init}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log_{+}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}}\left\|P_{\mathsf{init}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\|B_{\star}B_{\star}^{\mathsf{H}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}+\Sigma_{\mathbf{w}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)

2.

\frac{W_{\mathrm{tr}}}{\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}\Delta_{r}}\log_{+}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}\right]}{\delta\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}}\right)\left(d_{\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{tail},\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}}\right)

3.

\max\{1,\left\|K_{\mathsf{init}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\}\cdot\frac{d_{u}\left(\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}\right]+\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\log\left(\frac{d_{u}}{\delta}\right)\right)}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{stable}}^{2}}
.

Proof.

The overall proof strategy is to show there exists a threshold such that the error in both
A_{\star}
and
B_{\star}
is small. We deal with operator error separately. Assume
\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}},A_{0},
and
B_{0}
are computed as in
\mathsf{WarmStart}
(see
Appendix

F
). Furthermore, redefine
A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}:=A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{\mathsf{init}}
.

B_{\star}
recovery

This part is simple since we have consistent parameter recovery for
B_{\star}
. Recalling
Proposition

E.2
, the following statement holds with probability
1-\delta/2
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert B_{\star}-B_{0}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{d_{u}\left(\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}\right]+\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\log\left(\frac{d_{u}}{\delta}\right)\right)}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}T}.

Since
T_{\mathsf{init}}\gtrsim\max\{1,\left\|K_{\mathsf{init}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\}\frac{d_{u}\left(\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}\right]+\left\lVert\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\log\left(\frac{d_{u}}{\delta}\right)\right)}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathbf{u}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{stable}}^{2}}
we have that with probability
1-\delta/2
,

\|B_{\star}-B_{0}\|_{\mathsf{HS}}~\leq~\frac{1}{4\max\{1,\left\|K_{\mathsf{init}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{stable}}.

A_{\star}
recovery

In order to guarantee a close estimate of
A_{\star}
, we use the error decomposition from
Lemma

E.1
and show that this quantity is small in operator norm if the alignment condition holds. For the sake of making notation concise, we let,
\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}:=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}
and
A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}=A_{\star}+B_{\star}K_{\mathsf{init}}
. From our earlier error decomposition,

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\lambda A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}-\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle~\leq~2\underbrace{\left\|\lambda A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}}_{:=N_{1}}+2\underbrace{\left\lVert\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}}_{:=N_{2}}.

Bounding the noise

The bound on
N_{2}
follows a simple application of
Lemma

E.4
.

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle~\leq~\frac{1}{\lambda T^{2}}\left\lVert\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle~\leq~\frac{1}{\lambda T}\left\lVert\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(B_{\star}\mathbf{v}_{t}+\mathbf{w}_{t}\right)\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}\right)\left(T\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda TI\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}.

Applying
Lemma

E.4
, we get that with probability
1-\delta/4
,

\displaystyle N_{2}\lesssim\frac{W_{\mathrm{tr}}}{\lambda T}\cdot\log_{+}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}\right]}{\delta\lambda}\right)\left(d_{\lambda}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{tail},\lambda}\right).

Bounding the bias

The bound on
N_{1}
follows from the alignment condition. In order to apply it, we first perform the following simplification of the bias term.

\displaystyle\left\|\lambda A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle~\leq~\lambda\left\|A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda\left\|\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{\mathsf{H}}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

(E.6)

As in the alignment condition, we let
U(\Lambda_{r}+\Lambda_{/r})V^{\mathsf{H}}
be the SVD of
A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}
. Therefore,
A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}^{\mathsf{H}}A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}
is equal to
V(\Lambda_{r}^{2}+\Lambda_{/r}^{2})V^{\mathsf{H}}
. Applying the triangle inequality, we can then bound (
E.6
) by,

\displaystyle\lambda\left\|\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}V\Lambda_{r}^{2}V^{\mathsf{H}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\lambda\left\|\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}V\Lambda_{/r}^{2}V^{\mathsf{H}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

(E.7)

We can bound the second term above by
\|\Lambda_{/r}^{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=s_{r+1}^{2}
. Next, we have chosen
T_{\mathsf{init}}
large enough so that
\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I
is a PSD upper bound on
\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}
as per
Proposition

E.6
. Together with the alignment condition, we have that with probability
1-\delta/4
,

\displaystyle V\Lambda_{r}^{2}V^{\mathsf{H}}\preceq\rho\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}\preceq c\cdot\rho(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I),

for some universal constant
c
. This implies that,

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}V\Lambda_{r}^{2}V^{\mathsf{H}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}+\lambda I\right)^{-1/2}\preceq c\rho I,

and hence the first term in
Equation

E.7
is smaller than
c\cdot\lambda\rho
. Putting everything together, we get that with probability
1-\delta/4
,
N_{1}
is less than or equal to
c\rho\lambda+s_{r+1}^{2}
.
Therefore, with probability
1-\delta/2
,

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}~\leq~c_{0}\frac{W_{\mathrm{tr}}}{\lambda T}\cdot\log_{+}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{init}}\right]}{\delta\lambda}\right)\left(d_{\lambda}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{tail},\lambda}\right)+c_{1}\rho\lambda+s_{r+1}^{2}.

(E.8)

Now, we let
\Delta_{r}:=\frac{1}{16}\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{stable}}^{2}-s_{r+1}^{2}
which is strictly great than
0
by
3
. Setting
\lambda=\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}:=c^{\prime}\frac{\Delta_{r}}{\rho}
for some universal constant
c^{\prime}
, we have that
c_{1}\rho\lambda~\leq~\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{r}
. Furthermore, for
T
such that,

T\gtrsim\frac{W_{\mathrm{tr}}}{\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}\Delta_{r}}\log_{+}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma_{\mathbf{x},0}\right]}{\delta\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}}\right)\left(d_{\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{tail},\lambda_{\mathsf{safe}}}\right),

the first term in
Equation

E.8
is less than or equal to
\Delta_{r}/2
and hence
\|\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}~\leq~\frac{1}{16}\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{stable}}^{2}
. Computing
A_{0}=\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-B_{0}K_{\mathsf{init}}
, we get that:

\displaystyle\left\|A_{0}-A_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|(\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-B_{0}K_{\mathsf{init}})\pm A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}-A_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\left\|\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-A_{\mathrm{cl}_{\star}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\|(B_{\star}-B_{0})K_{\mathsf{init}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle~\leq~\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{stable}}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

E.4
Estimation Lemmas

Lemma E.8

(Theorem 2.6 in
Chen and Yang 2021
)
.

Let
\mathbf{v}_{i}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
be independent random vectors in a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
such that
\mathbf{v}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{i})
and
\Sigma_{i}\preceq\Sigma
for all
i
. Then,

\Pr\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}\|^{2}~\geq~2n\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma\right]+5t\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\right]~\leq~\exp\left(-t\right)

Proof.

The lemma is a restatement of Theorem 2.6 in
Chen and Yang 2021
. Since the
\mathbf{v}_{i}
are Gaussians, the inequality immediately following Equation 4.2 in the proof of Theorem 2.6 in
Chen and Yang 2021
can be restated as,

\Pr\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}\|^{2}~\geq~n\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma\right]+t\right]~\leq~\exp\left(-\lambda t+2n\lambda^{2}\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}\right)\text{ for all }0~\leq~\lambda<\frac{1}{4\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}.

Since
\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}~\leq~\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}
, if we set
\lambda=(5\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}})^{-1}
, we get that

\Pr\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}\|^{2}~\geq~n\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma\right]+t\right]~\leq~\exp\left(-\frac{t}{5\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}}+\frac{2}{25}n\right).

Lastly, setting
-t^{\prime}=-\frac{t}{5\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}}+\frac{2}{25}n
,

\Pr\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}\|^{2}~\geq~n\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma\right]+5t^{\prime}\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}+\frac{2}{5}n\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}\right]~\leq~\exp\left(-t^{\prime}\right).

The proof follows since
\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}~\leq~\mathrm{tr}\left[\Sigma\right]
.
∎

Lemma E.9

(Lemma E.4 in
Simchowitz and Foster 2020
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{t}
be a filtration such that
\mathbf{z}_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{{}_{t}})
where
\Sigma_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
-measurable, and
\Sigma_{t}\succeq\Sigma
. Furthermore, assume that

\mathbb{E}\;\mathrm{tr}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{z}_{t}\right]~\leq~M.

Then for,

T~\geq~223\max\left\{2d\log(\frac{100}{3})+d\log\left(\frac{M}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}\right),\;(d+1)\log\left(\frac{2}{\delta}\right)\right\},

with probability
1-\delta
,

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{z}_{t}\succeq\frac{9}{1600}\Sigma.

Lemma E.10

(Corollary 3.6 in
Abbasi-Yadkori 2012
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{F}_{k},k~\geq~1)
be a filtration and let
(\mathbf{m}_{k},k~\geq~1)
be an
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}
-valued stochastic process adapted to
\mathcal{F}_{k}
, and
(\eta_{k},k~\geq~2)
be a real valued martingale difference process adapted to
\mathcal{F}_{k}
. Furthermore, assume that
\eta_{k}
is conditionally sub-Gaussian in the sense that there exists a
\sigma>0
such that,
\mathbb{E}\left[\exp(\eta_{k}\gamma)\right]~\leq~\exp\left(\gamma^{2}\sigma/2\right)

for all
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
. Consider the martingale and operator-valued processes,

\displaystyle S_{t}:=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k+1}\cdot\mathbf{m}_{k},\quad V_{t}:=\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mathbf{m}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{m}_{k},\quad\overline{V}_{t}:=\lambda I+V_{t}\text{ for }t~\geq~0.

Then, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\forall t~\geq~2,\quad\|\overline{V}_{t}^{-1/2}S_{t}\|~\leq~2\sigma\log\left(\frac{\det(I+\lambda^{-1}V_{t})}{\delta}\right).

Part III
Regret Bounds

Appendix F
Algorithm Descriptions: OnlineCE and WarmStart

Below, we let
\mathcal{B}:=\{A_{\mathrm{cl}}:\left\|A_{\mathrm{cl}}-(A_{0}+B_{0}K_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}~\leq~.5\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{stable}}\}
denote an operator norm ball around the warm start estimate
A_{0}+B_{0}K_{0}
and let
\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{t}
be the empirical state covariance. The precise description of
T_{\mathsf{exp}}
and
\lambda
may be found in the proof of
Theorem

3.1
.

\mathsf{OnlineCE}

Input:
Warm start estimates
(A_{0},B_{0})
, confidence
\delta
, horizon length
T

1.

Synthesize controller
K_{0}=K_{\infty}(A_{0},B_{0})

2.

Collect data under exploration policy

For
t=1,2,\ldots,T_{\mathsf{exp}}:

•

Observe state
\mathbf{x}_{t}

•

Choose input
\mathbf{u}_{t}=K_{0}\mathbf{x}_{t}+\mathbf{v}_{t}
where
\mathbf{v}_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I)

3.

Estimate
B_{\star}

\widehat{B}=\argmin_{B}\sum_{t=1}^{T_{\mathsf{exp}}}\frac{1}{2T_{\mathsf{exp}}}\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{t+1}-B\mathbf{v}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}^{2}

4.

Estimate
A_{\star}

(a)

Compute initial estimate via ridge regression

\displaystyle\widetilde{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}

\displaystyle:=\textstyle\argmin_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\frac{1}{T_{\mathsf{exp}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T_{\mathsf{exp}}}\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{t+1}-A_{\mathrm{cl}}\mathbf{x}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\left\lVert A_{\mathrm{cl}}\right\rVert_{\mathsf{HS}}^{2}

(b)

Project to safe set

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}:=\argmin_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}\in\mathcal{B}}\left\langle(A_{\mathrm{cl}}-\widetilde{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}),(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{x},0}+\lambda I)(A_{\mathrm{cl}}-\widetilde{A_{\mathrm{cl}}})\right\rangle

(c)

Refine estimate

\widehat{A}:=\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{cl}}-\widehat{B}K_{0}

5.

Synthesize certainty equivalence controller
\widehat{K}=K_{\infty}(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})

6.

Choose inputs according to
\widehat{K}
for remainder of horizon.

For
t=T_{\mathsf{exp}}+1,\ldots,T:

•

Observe state
\mathbf{x}_{t}

•

Choose input
\mathbf{u}_{t}=\widehat{K}\mathbf{x}_{t}

\mathsf{WarmStart}

Input:
Initial controller
K_{\mathsf{init}}
which stabilizes
(A_{\star},B_{\star})
.

1.

Collect data under exploration policy

For
t=1,2,\ldots,T_{\mathsf{init}}:

•

Observe state
\mathbf{x}_{t}

•

Choose input
\mathbf{u}_{t}=K_{0}\mathbf{x}_{t}+\mathbf{v}_{t}
where
\mathbf{v}_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I)

2.

Estimate
B_{\star}

B_{0}=\argmin_{B}\sum_{t=1}^{T_{\mathsf{exp}}}\frac{1}{2T_{\mathsf{exp}}}\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{t+1}-B\mathbf{v}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x}}}^{2}

3.

Estimate
A_{\star}

(a)
